\operatorname{\iota}_{0}
and hence
\mathsf{pr}_{1}\,g=\mathsf{pr}_{1}\,h
.

There is a simple connection between this additive strength and the
tensorial strength of the monad
\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}
introduced in
[
Ehr23
]
,
Section 4, see also Section
3.1.1
in the present paper,
through the strong symmetric monoidal structure of
\oc{\_}
(Seely
isomorphisms).

Theorem 5.4
.

The following diagram commutes.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\oc{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0}}}\otimes{\oc{X_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oc({{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oc{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\left({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}{\oc{X_{0}}}}\otimes{\oc{X_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}({\oc{X_{0}}}\otimes{\oc{X_{1}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\oc({{X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{m}^{2}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0},X_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\oc{X_{1}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}})^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\partial}_{{X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\varphi}_{\oc{X_{0}},\oc{X_{1}}}^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{m}^{2}_{X_{0},X_{1}}}

and similarly for
\psi_{X_{0},X_{1}}^{1}
and
\operatorname{\varphi}_{X_{0},X_{1}}^{1}
.

Remember that
(\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}})^{!}
is the promotion of
\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}}\in\mathcal{L}(\oc({{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}}),\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\left({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}\right))
, so that actually
(\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}})^{!}=\oc({{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\iota}_{0}}})
.

Proof 5.5
.

By
3.2
we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\partial}_{{X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}}=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}{\mathsf{m}^{2}_{X_{0},X_{1}}})\,\operatorname{\mathsf{L}}_{\oc{X_{0}},\oc{X_{1}}}\,({\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{1}}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}}})^{-1}

so that

\displaystyle\operatorname{\partial}_{{X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}}

\displaystyle\,(\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}})^{!}\,\mathsf{m}^{2}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0},X_{1}}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}{\mathsf{m}^{2}_{X_{0},X_{1}}})\,\operatorname{\mathsf{L}}_{\oc{X_{0}},\oc{X_{1}}}\,({\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{1}}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}}})^{-1}\,\oc({{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\iota}_{0}}})\,\mathsf{m}^{2}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0},X_{1}}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}{\mathsf{m}^{2}_{X_{0},X_{1}}})\,\operatorname{\mathsf{L}}_{\oc{X_{0}},\oc{X_{1}}}\,({\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{1}}})({\oc({\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0}})}\otimes{\oc{\operatorname{\iota}_{0}}})

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}{\mathsf{m}^{2}_{X_{0},X_{1}}})\,\operatorname{\mathsf{L}}_{\oc{X_{0}},\oc{X_{1}}}\,({\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\iota}_{0}})\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby~\ref{ax:daxlin} }

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}{\mathsf{m}^{2}_{X_{0},X_{1}}})\,\tau\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\varphi}_{\oc{X_{0}},\oc{X_{1}}}^{1})\,\operatorname{\varphi}_{\oc{X_{0}},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\oc{X_{1}}}^{0}\,({\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\iota}_{0}})

\displaystyle\text{\hskip 10.22217pt\hskip 10.22217ptby definition of }\operatorname{\mathsf{L}}\text{ in~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Ehrhard23a}{}{}]},
Section~4}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}{\mathsf{m}^{2}_{X_{0},X_{1}}})\,\tau\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\varphi}_{\oc{X_{0}},\oc{X_{1}}}^{1})\,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}({\oc{X_{0}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\iota}_{0}})\,\operatorname{\varphi}_{\oc{X_{0}},\oc{X_{1}}}^{0}\,({\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\oc{X_{1}}})

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}{\mathsf{m}^{2}_{X_{0},X_{1}}})\,\tau\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\iota}_{0})\,\operatorname{\varphi}_{\oc{X_{0}},\oc{X_{1}}}^{0}\,({\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\oc{X_{1}}})

\displaystyle\text{\hskip 10.22217pt\hskip 10.22217ptsince }\operatorname{\varphi}^{0}\text{ is a strength of the monad }\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\text{ whose unit is }\operatorname{\iota}_{0}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}{\mathsf{m}^{2}_{X_{0},X_{1}}})\,\operatorname{\varphi}_{\oc{X_{0}},\oc{X_{1}}}^{0}\,({\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\oc{X_{1}}})

by one of the monad commutations.

.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oc{\left({\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}\right)}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}}

.

, the

) is equal to

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\left({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}\right)}\,(\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}})^{!}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby~\ref{ax:daxchain} }

\displaystyle=\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}}\,.

Lemma 5.8
.

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}}={\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}

Proof 5.9
.

Immediate consequence of the definitions.

Theorem 5.10
.

The natural morphisms
\psi^{0},\psi^{1}
are strengths for the
monad
(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}},\zeta,\theta)
on the category
{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}
.

This means that the following diagrams commute in
{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\left({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\zeta_{X_{0}}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta_{{X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{2}X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\left({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{2}\left({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\left({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\theta_{X_{0}}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{0}_{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X_{0},X_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\psi_{X_{0},X_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{{X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\mathrel{\&}{\top}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\left({X}\mathrel{\&}{\top}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{X,\top}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{pr}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\mathsf{pr}_{0}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X_{0}\mathrel{\&}X_{1}\mathrel{\&}X_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\left({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}\right)}\mathrel{\&}{X_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}X_{1}\mathrel{\&}X_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{0}_{X_{0},{X_{1}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{2}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{0}_{{X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}},X_{2}}}

where we keep the associativity isomorphisms of
\mathrel{\&}
implicit.

Proof 5.11
.

It suffices to prove the corresponding commutations in
\mathcal{L}
rather than
{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}
since all the involved morphisms are
images of morphisms in
\mathcal{L}
through
\mathsf{Lin}_{\mathord{\oc}}
, and this is quite
easy.

The commutativity of this strength takes a particularly strong form in
this setting.

Lemma 5.12
.

The following diagram commutes in
\mathcal{L}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}{X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}{X_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\left({\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}^{2}\left({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\left({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}^{2}\left({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0},X_{1}}^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{0}_{X_{0},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\psi^{1}_{X_{0},X_{1}}}

Proof 5.13
.

We prove that for each
j,k\in\{0,1\}
one has

\operatorname{\pi}_{k}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\psi^{1}_{X_{0},X_{1}})\,\psi^{0}_{X_{0},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}}=\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{k}\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}})\,\psi^{1}_{X_{0},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}}

which will prove our contention since

\operatorname{\pi}_{k}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\,\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}=\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{k}
. This
amounts to proving that

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{k}\,\psi^{1}_{X_{0},X_{1}}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\,\psi^{0}_{X_{0},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}}=\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\,\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{k}\,\psi^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0},X_{1}}

for which we apply Equations (
8
)
and (
9
). We have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\psi^{1}_{X_{0},X_{1}}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\psi^{0}_{X_{0},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}}

\displaystyle=\left({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\right)\,\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}}\right)={\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}=\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\psi^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0},X_{1}}

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\psi^{1}_{X_{0},X_{1}}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\psi^{0}_{X_{0},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}}

\displaystyle=\left({0}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\right)\,\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\mathrel{\&}{0}\right)=0=\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\psi^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0},X_{1}}

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\psi^{1}_{X_{0},X_{1}}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\psi^{0}_{X_{0},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}}

\displaystyle=\left({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\right)\,\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\mathrel{\&}{0}\right)={\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\mathrel{\&}{0}

\displaystyle=\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\mathrel{\&}{0}\right)\,\left({\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\right)=\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\psi^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0},X_{1}}

and the last case is symmetrical.

As a consequence

\tau\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\varphi}_{1}^{X_{0},X_{1}})\,\operatorname{\varphi}_{0}^{X_{0},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}}=\tau\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\varphi}_{0}^{X_{0},X_{1}})\,\operatorname{\varphi}_{1}^{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0},X_{1}}\in\mathcal{L}(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\left({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}\right))\,.

Thanks to our assumption that
\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}
preserves
\mathrel{\&}
strictly, in
the sense of (
4
), this morphism is actually
the identity.

Theorem 5.14
.

The morphism

\tau\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\psi^{1}_{X_{0},X_{1}})\,\psi^{0}_{X_{0},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}}=\tau\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}})\,\psi^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0},X_{1}}
is the identity morphism.

Proof 5.15
.

The first equation results from Lemma
5.12
. From
the proof of that lemma we get

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\tau\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\psi^{1}_{X_{0},X_{1}})\,\psi^{0}_{X_{0},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\psi^{1}_{X_{0},X_{1}})\,\psi^{0}_{X_{0},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}}

\displaystyle={\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}

and

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\tau\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\psi^{1}_{X_{0},X_{1}})\,\psi^{0}_{X_{0},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\psi^{1}_{X_{0},X_{1}})\,\psi^{0}_{X_{0},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}}+\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\psi^{1}_{X_{0},X_{1}})\,\psi^{0}_{X_{0},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}}

\displaystyle=\left({0}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\right)+\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\mathrel{\&}{0}\right)={\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}

by linearity of
\mathrel{\&}
on morphisms.

More generally given objects
X_{0},\dots,X_{n}
we have an additive strength
morphism

\displaystyle\psi^{i}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}{X_{i}}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n}))\,.

which is actually linear and comes from

\psi^{i}\in\mathcal{L}(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}{X_{i}}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n}))
. Up to the identification of

\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n})
with

\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}{X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}{X_{n}}
, this morphism of
\mathcal{L}
can
simply be written as

\displaystyle\psi^{i}=\operatorname{\iota}_{0}^{X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\iota}_{0}^{X_{i-1}}\mathrel{\&}X_{i}\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\iota}_{0}^{X_{i+1}}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\iota}_{0}^{X_{n}}\,.

(10)

When we will need to be explicit as to the list of
objects
X_{0},\dots,X_{n}
, we will write
\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,X_{n}}
instead of

\psi^{i}
.

Lemma 5.16
.

Let
i,l\in\{0,\dots,n\}
. If
i\not=l
we have

\displaystyle(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,X_{n}})\,\psi^{l}_{X_{0},\dots,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{i},\dots,X_{n}}=\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\psi^{l}_{X_{0},\dots,X_{n}})\,\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{l},\dots,X_{n}}\,.

And for any
i,l\in\{0,\dots,n\}
, we have

\displaystyle\tau\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,X_{n}})\,\psi^{l}_{X_{0},\dots,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{i},\dots,X_{n}}

\displaystyle=\tau\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\psi^{l}_{X_{0},\dots,X_{n}})\,\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{l},\dots,X_{n}}

\displaystyle\tau\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,X_{n}})\,\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{i},\dots,X_{n}}

\displaystyle=\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,X_{n}}\,(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\tau\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n})\,.

Proof 5.17
.

The proof of the first equation is exactly as the one of
Lemma
5.12
. In the case
l\not=i
, the second
equation follows from the first one and from

\tau\,\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}=\tau
and in the case
l=i
, it is
trivial. We prove the last equation. We have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\tau\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,X_{n}})\,\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{i},\dots,X_{n}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,X_{n}})\,\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{i},\dots,X_{n}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,X_{n}}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{i},\dots,X_{n}}

\displaystyle=X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}(\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{0})\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n}

and

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,X_{n}}\,(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\tau\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n})

\displaystyle=X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}(\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\tau)\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n}

\displaystyle=X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}(\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{0})\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n}\,.

Next we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\tau\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,X_{n}})\,\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{i},\dots,X_{n}}

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt=(\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{1}+\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{0})\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,X_{n}})\,\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{i},\dots,X_{n}}

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt=\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,X_{n}}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{i},\dots,X_{n}}+\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,X_{n}}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{i},\dots,X_{n}}

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt=(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n})\,(0\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}0)

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt\quad+(0\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}0)\,(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n})

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt=0\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}(\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{1}+\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{0})\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}0

Notice that
\mathrel{\&}
is not a multilinear operation on morphisms, so
in the last equality we are crucially using the fact that all
factors but the
i
th are equal to
0
in both summands. On the
other hand we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,X_{n}}\,(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\tau\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n})

\displaystyle=0\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}(\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\tau)\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}0

\displaystyle=0\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}(\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{1}+\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{0})\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}0

proving our contention by the fact that
\operatorname{\pi}_{0},\operatorname{\pi}_{1}
are jointly monic.

Given
f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n},Y)
, we define the
i
-th partial derivative of
f
as

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{i}f=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}f\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{i}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}{X_{i}}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y)
.

Theorem 5.18
.

Let
i,l\in\{0,\dots,n\}
. If
i\not=l
then

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{i}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{l}f=\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{l}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{i}f

so that for any
i,l\in\{0,\dots,n\}
we have

\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{i}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{l}f=\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{l}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{i}f
. Moreover

\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{i}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{i}f=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{i}f\mathrel{\circ}(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\theta\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n})
.

This is an immediate consequence of
Lemma
5.16
and of the naturality of

\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}
and of
\theta
in the category
{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}
.

Notice that the morphism
\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{i}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{l}f
in the statement of this result belongs to

{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X_{i}\mathrel{\&}X_{i+1}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X_{l}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y)

if
i<l
and to

{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{2}X_{i}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y)
if
i=l
.

Theorem 5.19
.

Let
f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}},Y)
so that

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X_{1}},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y)
. Then

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}f=\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}f=\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}f\,.

Proof 5.20
.

The second equation holds by Theorem
5.18
. Next
we have

\displaystyle\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}f

\displaystyle=\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}f\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}})

\displaystyle=\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{2}f\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}{\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}}}\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X_{0},X_{1}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}f\mathrel{\circ}\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}{\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}}}\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X_{0},X_{1}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}f

by Theorem
5.14
.

Remark 5.21
.

The intuitive meaning of this result is that the derivative of a
function acting on pairs is obtained as the sum of its partial
derivatives. This sum is computed by the
\theta
natural
transformation.

Given
e\in{\mathbb{N}}
we can more generally define a linear

\displaystyle\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,X_{n}}(e)\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}X_{i}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}X_{n})

by induction on
e
(we give only the definition for
n=1
, the
generalization is easy and not really required for our purpose):
we set
\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}}(0)=\operatorname{\mathsf{Id}}
and
\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}}(e+1)=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}}(e)\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{0}_{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}X_{0},X_{1}}
typed
as follows:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e+1}X_{0}\mathrel{\&}X_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e+1}X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e+1}X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e+1}X_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{0}_{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}X_{0},X_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\psi^{0}_{X_{0},X_{1}}(e)}

and similarly for
\psi^{1}(e)
. We can easily give a direct description of this morphism.

Lemma 5.22
.

\psi^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,X_{n}}(e)=\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e)\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}X_{i}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e)

where
\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e)\in\mathcal{L}(X,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}^{e}X)
is defined inductively by
\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e)=\operatorname{\mathsf{Id}}
and
\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e+1)=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}{\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e)})\,\operatorname{\iota}_{0}
.

That is, in
{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}
,
\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e+1)=(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e))\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\iota}_{0}
. The
proof is a straightforward induction.

Lemma 5.23
.

If
f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}},Y)
,
d\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
i\in\{0,1\}
,
we have
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{i}^{d}f=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}f\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{i}_{X_{0},X_{1}}(d)
.

Proof 5.24
.

Immediate consequence of the functoriality of
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}
in
{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}

and of the definition of
\psi^{i}_{X_{0},X_{1}}(d)
.

Lemma 5.25
.

If
d<e
we have
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e)=\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e-1)
and
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e)=0
.

Proof 5.26
.

By induction on
d
. Notice first that we are actually dealing with
linear morphisms so that we can do our computations in
\mathcal{L}
. For
the base case we have, since
e>0
:
\operatorname{\pi}_{i}\,\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e)=\operatorname{\pi}_{i}\,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}(\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e-1))\,\operatorname{\iota}_{0}=\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e-1)\,\operatorname{\pi}_{i}\,\operatorname{\iota}_{0}
and we have
\operatorname{\pi}_{i}\,\operatorname{\iota}_{0}=\boldsymbol{\delta}_{{i},{0}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Id}}
.

For the inductive case observe first that since
d+1<e
we have

e\geq 2
. Then

\displaystyle(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}^{d+1}\operatorname{\pi}_{i})\,\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e)

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}^{d+1}\operatorname{\pi}_{i})\,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}(\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e-1))\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\iota}_{0}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}((\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i})\,\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e-1))\,\operatorname{\iota}_{0}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}(\boldsymbol{\delta}_{{i},{0}}\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e-2))\,\operatorname{\iota}_{0}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby ind.~hypothesis}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\delta}_{{i},{0}}\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e-1)

as contended.

We generalize the canonical flip

\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}\in\mathcal{L}(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}^{2}X,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}^{2}X)
to an iso

\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(l)\in\mathcal{L}(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}^{l+2}X,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}^{l+2}X)
for each
l\in{\mathbb{N}}

defined inductively by

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(0)=\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}\text{\quad and\quad}\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(l+1)=\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(l))\,.

Lemma 5.27
.

Given
i_{0},\dots,i_{l+1}\in\{0,1\}
, one has

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{l+1}}\,\cdots\,\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{0}}\,\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(l)=\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{0}}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{l+1}}\,\cdots\,\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{1}}\,.

Proof 5.28
.

By induction on
l
. For
l=0
the property holds by the very
definition of
\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}
. Assume that the property holds for
l
and
let us prove it for
l+1
. So let
i_{0},\dots,i_{l+2}\in\{0,1\}
. We
have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{l+2}}\,\cdots\,\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{0}}\,\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(l+1)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{l+2}}\,\cdots\,\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{0}}\,\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(l))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{l+2}}\,\cdots\,\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{2}}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{0}}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{1}}\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(l)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{l+2}}\,\cdots\,\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{2}}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{0}}\,\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(l)\,\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{1}}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby nat.~of }\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{1}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{0}}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{l+2}}\,\cdots\,\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{2}}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{1}}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby ind.~hyp.}

This means that
\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(l)
implements a circular permutation of
length
l+2
on the indices.

Lemma 5.29
.

Let
f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}},Y)
and let
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Then

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{k+1}f,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{k+1}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}f}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{k+1}X}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{k+2}Y)
satisfy the relation

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{k+1}{f}=\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(k)\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{k+1}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}f}
.

Proof 5.30
.

For
k=0
, this is just Theorem
5.18
. For
the inductive step we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{k+2}{f}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{k+1}f\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby Theorem~\ref{th:partial-der-commute}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}(\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(k)\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{k+1}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}f)\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby ind.~hyp.}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(k)\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{k+2}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}f\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby def.~of }\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(k+1)\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{k+2}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}f\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby def.~of }\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(k+1)\,.

5.2.
Differentiation in the closed case

Since our purpose is to provide the categorical foundations of

\Lambda_{\mathsf{cd}}
, we require the category
\mathcal{L}
to be closed wrt. its SMC
structure.

Remember that we consider the isos

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}({Z}\mathrel{\&}{X})\simeq\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Z\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X
and

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}{({X}\Rightarrow{Y})}\simeq{X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y}
as identities: this is
our (
S
\otimes
-fun
) axiom of
[
Ehr23
]
and we assume that the
corresponding iso, which is

\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}})=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}((\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}})\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{0}_{{X}\Rightarrow{Y},X})\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}({X}\Rightarrow{Y}),{X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y})
, is the
identity morphism. With these identifications we have the following
equation.

Lemma 5.31
.

Let
f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({Z}\mathrel{\&}{X},Y)
so that
\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,{X}\Rightarrow{Y})
,
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Z}\mathrel{\&}{X},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y)
and
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}f)\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Z,{X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y})
. Then
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}f)=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}f)
.

Proof 5.32
.

More precisely we must prove that
\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}})\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}f)=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}f)
which
boils down to the naturality of
\psi^{0}
by simple computations
in the CCC
{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}
.

Lemma 5.33
.

The following diagram commutes in
\mathcal{L}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X}\otimes{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X}\otimes{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}({X}\otimes{Y})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{\iota}_{0}}\otimes{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\iota}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\varphi}_{0}^{X,Y}}

This is easily proven using as usual the fact that
\operatorname{\pi}_{0},\operatorname{\pi}_{1}

are jointly monic.

Lemma 5.34
.

The following diagram commutes in
{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y})}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{2}Y})}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{2}Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{1}_{{X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y},X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}

Proof 5.35
.

We need to come back to the definition of the functor
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}
. We
expand the definition of

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}:{({X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y})}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\to\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y
in
\mathcal{L}
.

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}})\,\operatorname{\partial}_{{\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{Y}\right)}\mathrel{\&}{X}}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\mathsf{ev}})\,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{{\oc{X}}\multimap{Y}}}\otimes{\oc{X}})\,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}\,\operatorname{\partial}_{{\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{Y}\right)}\mathrel{\&}{X}}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby def.~of }\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\mathsf{ev}})\,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{{\oc{X}}\multimap{Y}}}\otimes{\oc{X}})\,\operatorname{\mathsf{L}}_{\oc{\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{Y}\right),\oc{X}}}\,({\operatorname{\partial}_{{\oc{X}}\multimap{Y}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby~\ref{ax:daxwith} }

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\mathsf{ev}})\,\operatorname{\mathsf{L}}_{{\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{Y}\right),\oc{X}}}\,({\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{{\oc{X}}\multimap{Y}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\oc{X}})\,({\operatorname{\partial}_{{\oc{X}}\multimap{Y}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\mathsf{ev}})\,\operatorname{\mathsf{L}}_{{\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{Y}\right),\oc{X}}}\,({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{Y}\right)}}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby~\ref{ax:daxchain} }

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\mathsf{ev}})\,\tau\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\varphi}^{0}_{{\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{Y}\right)},{\oc{X}}})\,\operatorname{\varphi}^{1}_{{\oc{X}}\multimap{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y},\oc{X}}\,({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{Y}\right)}}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby def.~of }\operatorname{\mathsf{L}}

\displaystyle=\tau\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}^{2}\operatorname{\mathsf{ev}})\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\varphi}^{0}_{{\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{Y}\right)},{\oc{X}}})\,\operatorname{\varphi}^{1}_{{\oc{X}}\multimap{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y},\oc{X}}\,({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{Y}\right)}}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\tau\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\mathsf{ev}})\,\operatorname{\varphi}^{1}_{{\oc{X}}\multimap{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y},\oc{X}}\,({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{Y}\right)}}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}

by the identification

\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{Y}\right)=\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y}\right)
. On the other
hand we have

\displaystyle(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}})\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{{X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y},X}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\mathsf{ev}})\,\operatorname{\mathsf{L}}_{{\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y}\right),\oc{X}}}\,({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y}\right)}}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}\,\oc{\psi^{1}_{{X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y},X}}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptas above}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\mathsf{ev}})\,\operatorname{\mathsf{L}}_{{\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y}\right),\oc{X}}}\,({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y}\right)}}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}\,\oc{\left({\operatorname{\iota}_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X}\right)}

\displaystyle\text{\hskip 61.33301ptby def.~of }\psi^{1}_{{X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y},X}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\mathsf{ev}})\,\operatorname{\mathsf{L}}_{{\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y}\right),\oc{X}}}\,({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y}\right)}}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X}})\,{({\oc{\operatorname{\iota}_{0}}}\otimes{\oc{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X}})}\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\mathsf{ev}})\,\operatorname{\mathsf{L}}_{{\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y}\right),\oc{X}}}\,{({\operatorname{\iota}_{0}}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X}})}\,({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y}\right)}}\otimes{\oc{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\mathsf{ev}})\,\tau\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\varphi}^{1}_{{\oc{X}}\multimap{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y},\oc{X}})\,\operatorname{\varphi}^{0}_{{\oc{X}}\multimap{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\oc{X}}\,{({\operatorname{\iota}_{0}}\otimes{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\oc{X}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt\,{({\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y}\right)}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X}})}\,({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y}\right)}}\otimes{\oc{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby def.~of }\operatorname{\mathsf{L}}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\mathsf{ev}})\,\tau\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\varphi}^{1}_{{\oc{X}}\multimap{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y},\oc{X}})\,\operatorname{\iota}_{0}\,({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y}\right)}}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}\text{\ \ by Lemma~\ref{lemma:sstr-sin0}}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\mathsf{ev}})\,\tau\,\operatorname{\iota}_{0}\,\operatorname{\varphi}^{1}_{{\oc{X}}\multimap{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y},\oc{X}}\,({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y}\right)}}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\operatorname{\mathsf{ev}})\,\operatorname{\varphi}^{1}_{{\oc{X}}\multimap{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y},\oc{X}}\,({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y}\right)}}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}

which proves our contention.

5.3.
The case of multilinear morphisms

For each
i\in\{0,\dots,n\}
we can define a tensorial generalized
strength

\operatorname{\varphi}^{i}_{X_{0},\dots,X_{n}}\in\mathcal{L}(X_{0}\otimes\cdots\otimes\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}{X_{i}}\otimes\cdots\otimes X_{n},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}{(X_{0}\otimes\cdots\otimes X_{n})})\,.

Let
l\in\mathcal{L}(X_{0}\otimes\cdots\otimes X_{n},Y)
, then we define

\mathsf{m}(l)\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n},Y)
as the following
composition of morphisms
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oc({X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oc{X_{0}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\oc{X_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{0}\otimes\cdots\otimes X_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathsf{m}^{n})}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{0}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}

A morphism in
{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n},Y)
definable in that
way can be called an
n+1
-linear
morphism (that is, a
multilinear morphisms with
n+1
arguments) for the following reason.

Lemma 5.36
.

With these notations, we have

\mathsf{m}(l)\mathrel{\circ}(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{i-1}\mathrel{\&}0\mathrel{\&}X_{i+1}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n})=0

and if

f_{0},f_{1}\in\mathcal{L}(Z,X_{i})
are summable then so are

\mathsf{m}(l)\mathrel{\circ}(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{i-1}\mathrel{\&}f_{j}\mathrel{\&}X_{i+1}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n})

for
j=0,1
and

\mathsf{m}(l)\mathrel{\circ}(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{i-1}\mathrel{\&}(f_{0}+f_{1})\mathrel{\&}X_{i+1}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n})\\
=\mathsf{m}(l)\mathrel{\circ}(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{i-1}\mathrel{\&}f_{0}\mathrel{\&}X_{i+1}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n})\\
+\mathsf{m}(l)\mathrel{\circ}(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{i-1}\mathrel{\&}f_{1}\mathrel{\&}X_{i+1}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n})\,.

Proof 5.37
.

For the summability, we have

\mathsf{m}(l)\mathrel{\circ}(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{i-1}\mathrel{\&}f_{j}\mathrel{\&}X_{i+1}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n})=\mathsf{m}(l_{j})
where

l_{j}=l\,(X_{0}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{j}\otimes\cdots\otimes X_{n})
and
since
f_{0},f_{1}
are summable so are

X_{0}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{j}\otimes\cdots\otimes X_{n}
for
j=0,1
by (
S
\otimes
-dist
) of
[
Ehr23
]
with

X_{0}\otimes\cdots\otimes(f_{0}+f_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes X_{n}
as sum. The
result follows by
Lemma 12 of
[
Ehr23
]
. For the property relative to
0
we use similarly
the fact that
0
is absorbing for
\otimes
and for composition in

\mathcal{L}
.

Theorem 5.38
.

Let
f\in\mathcal{L}(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n},Y)
be
n+1
-linear. Then

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X_{n},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y)
is also
n+1
-linear.

Proof 5.39
.

We assume
n=1
for the sake of readability, the general case is not
more difficult. Let
l\in\mathcal{L}({X_{0}}\otimes{X_{1}},Y)
be such that
f=\mathsf{m}(l)
. Then we set
l^{\prime}=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}l)\,\operatorname{\mathsf{L}}_{X_{0},X_{1}}\in\mathcal{L}({\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0}}\otimes{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y)
and using
3.2
and
3.2
one shows that
\mathsf{m}(l^{\prime})=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}f
.

Lemma 5.40
.

Given
X_{0},\dots,X_{n}\in\mathsf{Obj(\mathcal{L})}
and
X=X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\operatorname{\partial}_{X}\,\mathsf{m}^{n}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}^{n}\,(\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}})

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\operatorname{\partial}_{X}\,\mathsf{m}^{n}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}^{n}\,((\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{0}})\otimes\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}+\cdots+\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\otimes(\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{0}}))\,.

which both belong to

\mathcal{L}(\oc{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}{X_{0}}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\oc{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}{X_{n}}},\oc{X})
.

Proof 5.41
.

For the sake of readability we assume that
n=1
, the general case
is not more difficult. By Axiom
3.2
we have

\operatorname{\partial}_{X}\,\mathsf{m}^{2}=\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{m}^{2}\,\operatorname{\mathsf{L}}_{2}\,({\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{1}}})

hence

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\operatorname{\partial}_{X}\,\mathsf{m}^{2}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{m}^{2}\,\operatorname{\mathsf{L}}_{2}\,({\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{1}}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}^{2}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\operatorname{\mathsf{L}}_{2}\,({\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{1}}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}^{2}\,({\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\otimes{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}})\,({\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{1}}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}^{2}({\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}}\otimes{\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}})

and

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\operatorname{\partial}_{X}\,\mathsf{m}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}^{2}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\operatorname{\mathsf{L}}_{2}\,({\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{1}}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}^{2}\,({\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\otimes{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}+{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\otimes{\operatorname{\pi}_{1}})\,({\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{1}}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}^{2}\,({(\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{0}})}\otimes{\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}}+{\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}}\otimes{(\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{1}})})\,.

Theorem 5.42
.

With the same notations,

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\mathsf{m}(l)\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X_{n},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y)
satisfies

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\mathsf{m}(l)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}(l)\mathrel{\circ}(\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\pi}_{0})

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\mathsf{m}(l)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}(l)\mathrel{\circ}(\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\pi}_{0})+\cdots+\mathsf{m}(l)\mathrel{\circ}(\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\pi}_{1})\,.

Proof 5.43
.

Immediate consequence of Lemma
5.40
and of
the naturality of the
\operatorname{\pi}_{j}
’s wrt.
\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}
in
\mathcal{L}
.

For
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, we have

(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}l)\,\operatorname{\varphi}^{i}\in\mathcal{L}(X_{0}\otimes\cdots\otimes\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}{X_{i}}\otimes\cdots\otimes X_{n},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y)
. Remember that
\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\in\mathcal{L}(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X,X)
. Given a
“linear” morphism
h\in\mathcal{L}(X,Y)
, we use the same notation
h
for
the corresponding morphism
\mathsf{Lin}_{\mathord{\oc}}h=h\,\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(X,Y)
.

Let
l\in\mathcal{L}(X_{0}\otimes\cdots\otimes X_{n},Y)
, since
\mathsf{m}(l)
is
multilinear, its partial derivatives should be “trivial”, the
purpose of the next result is to state precisely this
triviality. Given
i\in\{0,\dots,n\}
, we define the
i
-th
“partial application” of the functor
\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}
to
l
as

\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}_{i}l=(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}l)\,\operatorname{\varphi}^{i}\in\mathcal{L}(X_{0}\otimes\cdots\otimes\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{i}\otimes\cdots\otimes X_{n},\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}Y)
.

Theorem 5.44
.

For each
i\in\{0,\dots,n\}
we have

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{i}\mathsf{m}(l)=\mathsf{m}(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}_{i}l)
and for

j\in\{0,1\}
, we have

\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{i}\mathsf{m}(l)=\mathsf{m}(l)\mathrel{\circ}(X_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}X_{n})
.

Proof 5.45
.

For the sake of readability we take
n=1
. The general case is
conceptually not more difficult to deal with, just harder to read
due to cumbersome notations. We first prove the second equation for

i=1
(the case
i=0
is similar), namely, in
\mathcal{L}
:

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}\mathsf{m}(l)=\mathsf{m}(l)\,\oc{({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{j}})}

where
l\in\mathcal{L}({X_{0}}\otimes{X_{1}},Y)
so that

\mathsf{m}(l)=l\,({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{1}}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}
is a
bilinear morphism in
{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}
. We have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}\mathsf{m}(l)=\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{m}(l)\,\operatorname{\partial}_{{X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}}\,\oc{\psi^{1}}=\mathsf{m}(l)\,\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\,\operatorname{\partial}_{{X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{X_{1}}}\,\oc{\psi^{1}}\,.

Therefore

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}\mathsf{m}(l)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}(l)\,\oc{\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\right)}\,\oc{\psi^{1}}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby~\ref{ax:daxlocal} }

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}(l)\,\oc{\left({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\right)}

since

\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\right)\,\psi^{1}=\left({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\right)
. Next

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}\mathsf{m}(l)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}(l)\,\mathsf{m}^{2}\,(({(\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{0}})}\otimes{\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}})+({\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}}\otimes{(\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{1}})}))\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}\,\oc{\psi^{1}}

\displaystyle\text{\hskip 61.33301ptby~\ref{ax:daxwith} and def.~of }\operatorname{\mathsf{L}}

\displaystyle=l\,({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{1}}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}\,\mathsf{m}^{2}\,(({(\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{0}})}\otimes{\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}})+({\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}}\otimes{(\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{1}})}))\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}\,\oc{\psi^{1}}

\displaystyle\text{\hskip 61.33301ptby def.~of }\mathsf{m}(l)

\displaystyle=l\,({(\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{0}}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{0}})}\otimes{(\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{1}}\,\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}})}+{(\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{0}}\,\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}})}\otimes{(\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{1}}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{1}})})\,\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}\,\oc{\psi^{1}}

\displaystyle=l\,({(\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{0}}}\,\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{0}})}\otimes{(\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{1}}\,\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt+{(\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{0}}\,\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}})}\otimes{(\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{1}}}\,\operatorname{\partial}_{X_{1}})})\,\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}\,\oc{\psi^{1}}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby nat.~of }\operatorname{\pi}_{1}

\displaystyle=l\,({(\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{0}})}\otimes{(\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{1}}\,\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}})}+{(\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{0}}\,\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}})}\otimes{(\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\,\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}})})\,\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}\,\oc{\psi^{1}}

by
3.2

\displaystyle=l\,({(\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{0}}\,\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{1}})}\otimes{(\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{1}}\,\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}})}+{(\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{0}}\,\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}})}\otimes{(\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{1}}\,\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{1}})})\,\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}\,\oc{\psi^{1}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}(l)\,\mathsf{m}^{2}\,({\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}}\otimes{\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}}+{\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}}\otimes{\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}})\,\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}\,\oc{\psi^{1}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}(l)\,(\oc{\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\right)}+\oc{\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\right)})\,\oc{\psi^{1}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}(l)\,\oc{\left({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\right)}+\mathsf{m}(l)\,\oc{\left({0}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\right)}

since
\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\right)\psi^{1}=\left({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\right)
and

\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\right)\psi^{1}=\left({0}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\right)
. Finally we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{m}(l)\,\oc{\left({0}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\right)}

\displaystyle=l\,({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{1}}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}\,\oc{\left({0}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\right)}

\displaystyle=l\,({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{1}}})\,({\oc{0}}\otimes{\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}

\displaystyle=l\,({0}\otimes{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}=0

proving our contention. Now we prove the first equation for
i=1

(the case
i=0
is similar). For
j\in\{0,1\}
we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\,\mathsf{m}((\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}l)\,\operatorname{\varphi}^{1})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\,(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}l)\,\operatorname{\varphi}^{1}\,({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}

\displaystyle=l\,\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\,\operatorname{\varphi}^{1}\,({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}

\displaystyle=l\,({X_{0}}\otimes{\operatorname{\pi}_{j}})\,({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}X_{1}}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby def.~of }\operatorname{\varphi}^{1}

\displaystyle=l\,({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{1}}})\,({\oc{X_{0}}}\otimes{\oc{\operatorname{\pi}_{j}}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}

\displaystyle=l\,({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{0}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{X_{1}}})\,({\mathsf{m}^{2}})^{-1}\,\oc{\left({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{j}}\right)}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}(l)\,\oc{\left({X_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{j}}\right)}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby definition of }\mathsf{m}(l)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}\mathsf{m}(l)\text{\hskip 10.22217ptas we have just proven,}

which proves our first equation by the fact that
\operatorname{\pi}_{0},\operatorname{\pi}_{1}

are jointly epic.

5.4.
The basic multilinear constructs

Now we introduce the multilinear operations which will interpret the
basic constructs of
\Lambda_{\mathsf{cd}}
.
We make the following assumption about
\mathcal{L}
.

(
Int
)
The functor
X\mapsto{1}\oplus{X}
from
\mathcal{L}
to
\mathcal{L}
has an initial
algebra
\mathsf{N}
.

This means that there is a morphism

\chi\in\mathcal{L}({1}\oplus{\mathsf{N}},\mathsf{N})
such that for any

f\in\mathcal{L}({1}\oplus{X},X)
there is exactly one morphism

g\in\mathcal{L}(\mathsf{N},X)
such that
f\,({1}\oplus{g})=g\,\chi
.
We know that there is only one such morphism
\chi
, and that this
morphism is an iso (Lambek’s Lemma). We assume that
\chi
is the
identity to simplify notations, so that
\mathsf{N}={1}\oplus{\mathsf{N}}
“on
the nose”.
Given
f\in\mathcal{L}({1}\oplus{X},X)
we use
\overline{\mathsf{it}}(f)
for the unique
element of
\mathcal{L}(\mathsf{N},X)
such that

\overline{\mathsf{it}}(f)=f\,({1}\oplus{\overline{\mathsf{it}}(f)})
.

We set
\overline{\mathsf{suc}}=\overline{\pi}_{1}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathsf{N},{1}\oplus{\mathsf{N}})=\mathcal{L}(\mathsf{N},\mathsf{N})

which represents the successor constructor on integers and

\overline{\mathsf{zero}}=\overline{\pi}_{0}\in\mathcal{L}(1,\mathsf{N})
which represents the zero
constant.
It follows that for each
\nu\in{\mathbb{N}}
we can define the constants

\overline{\nu}\in\mathcal{L}(1,\mathsf{N})
by
\overline{0}=\overline{\mathsf{zero}}
and

\overline{\nu+1}=\overline{\mathsf{suc}}{(\overline{\nu})}
.

Next we define the predecessor morphism

\overline{\mathsf{pred}}=\left[{\overline{\pi}_{0},\mathsf{N}}\right]\in\mathcal{L}({1}\oplus{\mathsf{N}},\mathsf{N})
, that is

\overline{\mathsf{pred}}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathsf{N},\mathsf{N})
.
We have
\overline{\mathsf{pred}}\,\overline{0}=\overline{0}
and

\overline{\mathsf{pred}}\,\overline{\nu+1}=\overline{\nu}
.

Next notice that we have a morphism

h_{\mathsf{N}}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathsf{N},\oc{\mathsf{N}})
which turns
\mathsf{N}
into a

\oc{\_}
-coalgebra (that is, an object of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\oc}
, the
Eilenberg-Moore category of the comonad
\oc{\_}
).
This morphism is
h_{\mathsf{N}}=\overline{\mathsf{it}}(f)
where

f=\left[{\oc{\overline{\pi}_{0}}\,h_{1},\oc{\mathsf{N}}}\right]\in\mathcal{L}({1}\oplus{\oc{\mathsf{N}}},\oc{\mathsf{N}})
where

h_{1}=\oc{({\mathsf{m}^{0}})^{-1}}\,\operatorname{\mathsf{dig}}_{\top}\,\mathsf{m}^{0}\in\mathcal{L}(1,\oc{1})

is the canonical
\oc{\_}
-coalgebra structure of
1
.
This allows one in particular to define an erasing morphism

\mathsf{w}_{\mathsf{N}}=\operatorname{\mathsf{weak}}_{\mathsf{N}}\,h_{\mathsf{N}}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathsf{N},1)
as well
as a duplicating morphism

\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{N}}=({\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{\mathsf{N}}}\otimes{\operatorname{\mathsf{der}}_{\mathsf{N}}})\,\operatorname{\mathsf{contr}}_{\mathsf{N}}\,h_{\mathsf{N}}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathsf{N},{\mathsf{N}}\otimes{\mathsf{N}})
.
Remember indeed that, for any object
X
of
\mathcal{L}
, the object

\oc{X}
has a canonical structure of cocommutative comonoid with
counit
\operatorname{\mathsf{weak}}_{X}\in\mathcal{L}(\oc{X},1)
and comultiplication

\operatorname{\mathsf{contr}}_{X}\in\mathcal{L}(\oc{X},{\oc{X}}\otimes{\oc{X}})
.

Given an object
X
we set

\overline{\mathsf{let}}=\operatorname{\mathsf{ev}}\,\gamma\,({h_{\mathsf{N}}}\otimes{({\oc{\mathsf{N}}}\multimap{X})})\in\mathcal{L}({\mathsf{N}}\otimes{({\oc{\mathsf{N}}}\multimap{X})},X)
.

Last we define

\overline{\mathsf{if}}=\operatorname{\mathsf{ev}}({g}\otimes{\mathsf{N}})\in\mathcal{L}(\mathsf{N}\otimes({X}\mathrel{\&}{X}),X)
where

g=\left[{\operatorname{\mathsf{cur}}(\mathsf{pr}_{0}\,\lambda),\operatorname{\mathsf{cur}}(\mathsf{pr}_{1}\,\lambda)\,\mathsf{w}_{\mathsf{N}}}\right]\in\mathcal{L}(\mathsf{N},{{X}\mathrel{\&}{X}}\multimap{X})

where
\mathsf{pr}_{j}\,\lambda
is typed as follows
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{1}\otimes{\left({X}\mathrel{\&}{X}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left({X}\mathrel{\&}{X}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{pr}_{j}}

so that the two following diagrams commute

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{1}\otimes{\left({X}\mathrel{\&}{X}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{N}}\otimes{\left({X}\mathrel{\&}{X}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X}\mathrel{\&}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overline{\mathsf{zero}}}\otimes{\left({X}\mathrel{\&}{X}\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathsf{if}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{pr}_{0}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{N}}\otimes{\left({X}\mathrel{\&}{X}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{N}}\otimes{\left({X}\mathrel{\&}{X}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{1}\otimes{\left({X}\mathrel{\&}{X}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X}\mathrel{\&}{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overline{\mathsf{suc}}}\otimes{\left({X}\mathrel{\&}{X}\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{w}_{\mathsf{N}}}\otimes{\left({X}\mathrel{\&}{X}\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathsf{if}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{pr}_{1}}

(11)

5.5.
Syntactic constructs in the model

We introduce now semantic constructs on morphisms which exactly
mimic the syntax so as to make the translation from syntax to
semantics straightforward.

First, given
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
we also use the notation
\overline{n}
for the
morphism
\overline{n}\,\operatorname{\mathsf{weak}}_{Z}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,\mathsf{N})
.

Given
f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,\mathsf{N})
we define

\overline{\mathsf{suc}}(f)=\overline{\mathsf{suc}}\,f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,\mathsf{N})
and similarly

\overline{\mathsf{pred}}(f)=\overline{\mathsf{pred}}\,f
.

More generally given
d\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\mathsf{N})
we
set
\overline{\mathsf{suc}}^{d}(f)=(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\overline{\mathsf{suc}})\mathrel{\circ}f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\mathsf{N})
. We define similarly
\overline{\mathsf{pred}}^{d}(f)\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\mathsf{N})
.

We have defined
\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\mathsf{N}\mathrel{\&}\left({X}\mathrel{\&}{X}\right),X)
. So we have

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{d}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\mathsf{N}\mathrel{\&}\left({X}\mathrel{\&}{X}\right),\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}X)

(notice that this is not a trilinear morphism, but a bilinear one,
separately linear in
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{k}\mathsf{N}
and
{X}\mathrel{\&}{X}
).
Let
g\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\mathsf{N})
and
f_{j}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,X)
for
j=0,1
.
We set

\overline{\mathsf{if}}^{d}(g,f_{0},f_{1})={\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{d}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{g,f_{0},f_{1}}\rangle\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{k}X)\,.

Notice that

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{d}{\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})}=\mathsf{m}(\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}_{0}^{d}\overline{\mathsf{if}})
(this notation is introduced in Section
5.3
).

We have defined
\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{let}})\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({\mathsf{N}}\mathrel{\&}{({\mathsf{N}}\Rightarrow{X})},X)
so that

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{d}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{let}})\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\mathsf{N}}\mathrel{\&}{({\mathsf{N}}\Rightarrow{X})},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}X)\,.

Let
g\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\mathsf{N})
and
f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({Z}\mathrel{\&}{\mathsf{N}},X)
so that
\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,{\mathsf{N}}\Rightarrow{X})
, we set

\overline{\mathsf{let}}^{d}(g,f)=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{d}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{let}})\mathrel{\circ}\langle{g,\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}f}\rangle\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}X)\,.

If
f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,{X}\Rightarrow{Y})
and
g\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,X)
then we define

({f})\,{g}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,Y)
as
({f})\,{g}=\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{f,g}\rangle
.

If
f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,{X}\Rightarrow{Y})
we have

\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\left({f}\mathrel{\&}{X}\right)\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({Z}\mathrel{\&}{X},Y)
and hence

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\left({f}\mathrel{\&}{X}\right))\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({Z}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y)
so that we set

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{cur}}(f)=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}{(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\left({f}\mathrel{\&}{X}\right)))}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y})\,.

Notice that

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\left({f}\mathrel{\&}{X}\right))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\left({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}f}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\right)\mathrel{\circ}\psi_{Z,X}^{1}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\psi_{{X}\Rightarrow{Y},X}^{1}\mathrel{\circ}({f}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X})

by naturality of
\psi^{1}
and hence

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{cur}}(f)=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{int}}^{X,Y}\mathrel{\circ}f

(12)

where

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{int}}^{X,Y}=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}{(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\psi_{{X}\Rightarrow{Y},X}^{1})}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({X}\Rightarrow{Y},{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y})

is the “internalization” of the functor
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}
made possible by
its strength.

Remember that a morphism
f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(X,Y)
is linear if
f=\mathsf{Lin}_{\mathord{\oc}}g
for some
g\in\mathcal{L}(X,Y)
and that, when this
g
exists, it is unique.

Lemma 5.46
.

The morphism

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{int}}^{X,Y}=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}{(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\psi_{{X}\Rightarrow{Y},X}^{1})}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({X}\Rightarrow{Y},{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y})
is linear.

Proof 5.47
.

This results from the fact that
\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}
is left-linear and
\psi^{1}_{{X}\Rightarrow{Y},X}
is linear on
{({X}\Rightarrow{Y})}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}
.

So we shall also consider tacitly
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{int}}
as an element of
\mathcal{L}({X}\Rightarrow{Y},{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y})
.

If
f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X)
and
j\in\{0,1\}
we set

\operatorname{\pi}_{j}(f)=\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\,f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,X)
, and if

f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{2}X)
we set

\tau(f)=\tau\,f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X)
and

\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(f)=\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}\,f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{2}X)
. Last if

f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,X)
we set

\operatorname{\iota}_{j}(f)=\operatorname{\iota}_{j}\,f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y)
.

Lemma 5.48
.

For any object
X
of
\mathcal{L}
we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{X}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\mathsf{N}\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\left({X}\mathrel{\&}{X}\right),\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X)

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{let}})_{X}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{let}})_{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\mathsf{N}\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}({\mathsf{N}}\Rightarrow{X}),\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X)

Proof 5.49
.

We have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{X}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}{\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{X}}\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{\mathsf{N},{X}\mathrel{\&}{X}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{X}\mathrel{\circ}{\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\right)}\right)}\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{\mathsf{N},{X}\mathrel{\&}{X}}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby Theorem~\ref{th:sproj-sdfun-mlin}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{X}\mathrel{\circ}{\left({\mathsf{N}}\mathrel{\&}{\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\right)}\right)}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\mathrel{\circ}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}

by naturality of
\overline{\mathsf{if}}_{X}
with respect to
X
. Next

\displaystyle\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{X}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}{\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{X}}\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{\mathsf{N},{X}\mathrel{\&}{X}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{X}\mathrel{\circ}{\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\mathrel{\&}{\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\right)}\right)}\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{\mathsf{N},{X}\mathrel{\&}{X}}

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt+\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{X}\mathrel{\circ}{\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\right)}\right)}\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{\mathsf{N},{X}\mathrel{\&}{X}}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby Theorem~\ref{th:sproj-sdfun-mlin}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{X}\mathrel{\circ}{\left({0}\mathrel{\&}{\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\right)}\right)}+\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{X}\mathrel{\circ}{\left({\mathsf{N}}\mathrel{\&}{\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\right)}\right)}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{X}\mathrel{\circ}{\left({\mathsf{N}}\mathrel{\&}{\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\right)}\right)}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby bilinearity of }\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby naturality}

and the contention follows by joint monicity of

\operatorname{\pi}_{0},\operatorname{\pi}_{1}
. The case of
\overline{\mathsf{let}}
is completely similar.

Lemma 5.50
.

For
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{k+1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{X}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(k)\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{k+1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{k+1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{let}})_{X}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(k)\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{k+1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{let}})_{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\,.

Proof 5.51
.

By Lemma
5.29
and Lemma
5.48
.

5.5.1.
Recursion

From now on we assume that
\mathcal{L}
is a differential summable resource
category which is Scott, see Section
3.2.3
.

Theorem 5.52
.

For any object
X
we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\mathcal{Y}^{X}=\mathcal{Y}^{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}{(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}})}

Observe that this equation is well typed: we have
\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}:{({X}\Rightarrow{X})}\mathrel{\&}{X}\to X
and hence
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}:{({X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X})}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\to\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X
so that
\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}):({X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X})\to({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X})

and hence both sides of the equation are morphisms
({X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X})\to\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X
.

Proof 5.53
.

By induction on
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
we prove that
\forall n\in{\mathbb{N}}\ \operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\mathcal{Y}^{X}_{n}=\mathcal{Y}^{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}_{n}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}{(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}})}
and the
result follows by continuity. For
n=0
the equation is obvious so
assume that it holds for some
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}^{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}_{n+1}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}{(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}})}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}^{X,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{{X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X},\mathcal{Y}^{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}_{n}}\rangle\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}{(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}^{X,X})}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}^{X,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}^{X,X}),\mathcal{Y}^{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}_{n}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}^{X,X})}\rangle

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}^{X,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}^{X,X}),\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\mathcal{Y}^{X}_{n}}\rangle\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby ind.~hyp.}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}^{X,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\mathrel{\circ}\left({\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}^{X,X})}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\right)\mathrel{\circ}\langle{{X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\mathcal{Y}^{X}_{n}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}^{X,X}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{{X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\mathcal{Y}^{X}_{n}}\rangle\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby cart.~closedness}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{{X}\Rightarrow{X},\mathcal{Y}^{X}_{n}}\rangle)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\mathcal{Y}^{X}_{n+1}

as contended, using also the fact that
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}
is a functor which
commutes with cartesian products.

Remark 5.54
.

This theorem is a remarkable property of the fixpoint operator at
any type
X
: its derivative can be simply expressed by means of
a fixpoint operator at type
\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{X}
.

5.6.
Interpreting types and terms

The translation of any type
A
into an object
\llbracket{A}\rrbracket
of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}

(that is, of
\mathcal{L})
is given by
\llbracket{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{\iota}}\rrbracket=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\mathsf{N}
and
\llbracket{{A}\Rightarrow{B}}\rrbracket=({\llbracket{A}\rrbracket}\Rightarrow{\llbracket{B}\rrbracket})
so that
\llbracket{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{A}}\rrbracket=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\llbracket{A}\rrbracket
holds for all type
A
and all

d\in{\mathbb{N}}
thanks to our identification of
{X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y}
with

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}({X}\Rightarrow{Y})
.

A context
\Gamma=(x_{1}:A_{1},\dots,x_{k}:A_{k})
is interpreted as
\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket=\llbracket{A_{1}}\rrbracket\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\llbracket{A_{k}}\rrbracket
considered as an
object of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}
.

The next theorem also provides our definition of the interpretation of
terms.

Theorem 5.55
.

Given a term
M
, a type
A
and a context
\Gamma
such that
{\Gamma}\vdash{M}:{A}
for some typing derivation
\delta
(so that
A
is
actually determined by
M
) one can define
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\llbracket{A}\rrbracket)
in
such a way that

•

\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\llbracket{A}\rrbracket)
depends only on

M
and not on
\delta

•

and if
M=M_{0}+M_{1}
then
\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
are summable in
{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\llbracket{A}\rrbracket)
and
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
(this makes
sense by Lemma
4.19
).

Proof 5.56
.

By induction on
\mathsf{sz}(\delta)
where
\delta
is a derivation of
the typing judgment
{\Gamma}\vdash{M}:{A}
.
We proceed by cases, according to the last rule in
\delta
