.

\blacktriangleright
If
M=x_{i}
for some
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
we set
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\mathsf{pr}_{i}
.

\blacktriangleright
If
M=\lambda x^{B}\,{N}
then we have
A=({B}\Rightarrow{C})
and

{\Gamma,x:B}\vdash{N}:{C}
so that by inductive hypothesis
\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:B}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket}\mathrel{\&}{\llbracket{B}\rrbracket},\llbracket{C}\rrbracket)

and we set
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}{\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:B}}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,{\llbracket{B}\rrbracket}\Rightarrow{\llbracket{C}\rrbracket})
by inductive
hypothesis.

\blacktriangleright
If
M=({N})\,{P}
with
{\Gamma}\vdash{N}:{{B}\Rightarrow{A}}
and
{\Gamma}\vdash{P}:{B}
then we have by inductive hypothesis
\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,{\llbracket{B}\rrbracket}\Rightarrow{\llbracket{A}\rrbracket})
and
\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\llbracket{B}\rrbracket)
and hence we set
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=({\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}})\,{\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}}=\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}}\rangle\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\llbracket{A}\rrbracket)
.

\blacktriangleright
If
M=\mathsf{Y}N
with
{\Gamma}\vdash{N}:{{A}\Rightarrow{A}}
so that by inductive hypothesis
\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,{\llbracket{A}\rrbracket}\Rightarrow{\llbracket{A}\rrbracket})
and
so we set
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\mathcal{Y}^{\llbracket{A}\rrbracket}\mathrel{\circ}\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\llbracket{A}\rrbracket)

as required.

\blacktriangleright
If
M=\underline{n}
for some
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we set
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\overline{n}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\mathsf{N})
.

\blacktriangleright
If
M=\operatorname{\mathsf{succ}}^{d}(N)
so that
{\Gamma}\vdash{N}:{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{\iota}}
and hence
\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\mathsf{N})
by inductive
hypothesis, we set
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\overline{\mathsf{suc}}^{d}(\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma})\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\mathsf{N})
.
Of course we set similarly
\llbracket{\operatorname{\mathsf{pred}}^{d}(N)}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\overline{\mathsf{pred}}^{d}(\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma})\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\mathsf{N})
.

\blacktriangleright
If
M=\mathsf{if}^{d}(N,P_{0},P_{1})
with
{\Gamma}\vdash{N}:{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{\iota}}
and
{\Gamma}\vdash{P_{j}}:{A}
for
j=0,1
so that by inductive hypothesis
\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\mathsf{N})
and
\llbracket{P_{j}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\llbracket{A}\rrbracket)
for
j=0,1
.
So we set
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\overline{\mathsf{if}}^{d}(\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\llbracket{P_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\llbracket{P_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma})\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\llbracket{A}\rrbracket=\llbracket{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{A}}\rrbracket)
where we use the notation
\overline{\mathsf{if}}^{d}
introduced in
Section
5.5
.

\blacktriangleright
If
M=\mathsf{let}^{d}(x,N,P)
with
{\Gamma}\vdash{N}:{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{\iota}}
and
{\Gamma,x:\iota}\vdash{P}:{A}
so that by inductive hypothesis
\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\mathsf{N})
and
\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\iota}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket}\mathrel{\&}{\mathsf{N}},\llbracket{A}\rrbracket)
and we set
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\overline{\mathsf{let}}^{d}(\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}{\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\iota}})\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\llbracket{A}\rrbracket_{\Gamma})

where we use the notation
\overline{\mathsf{let}}^{d}
introduced in
Section
5.5
.

\blacktriangleright
We set
\llbracket{0^{A}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=0\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\llbracket{A}\rrbracket)
.

\blacktriangleright
If
M=\operatorname{\pi}_{i}^{d}(N)
then
{\Gamma}\vdash{N}:{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d+1}{B}}
with
A=\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{B}
so that
\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d+1}\llbracket{B}\rrbracket)
and we
set
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i}\mathrel{\circ}\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\llbracket{B}\rrbracket=\llbracket{A}\rrbracket)
.

\blacktriangleright
If
M=\operatorname{\iota}_{i}^{d}(N)
then we have
{\Gamma}\vdash{N}:{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{B}}
with
A=\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d+1}{B}
so that
\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\llbracket{B}\rrbracket)
and we set
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\iota}_{i}\mathrel{\circ}\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d+1}\llbracket{B}\rrbracket=\llbracket{A}\rrbracket)
.

\blacktriangleright
If
M=\theta^{d}(N)
then we have
{\Gamma}\vdash{N}:{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d+1}{B}}
with
A=\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{B}
so that
\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d+1}\llbracket{B}\rrbracket)
and we
set
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\theta\mathrel{\circ}\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\llbracket{B}\rrbracket=\llbracket{A}\rrbracket)
.

\blacktriangleright
If
M=\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}^{d}_{l}(N)
then we have
{\Gamma}\vdash{N}:{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d+l+2}{B}}
with
A=\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d+l+2}{B}
and
\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d+l+2}\llbracket{B}\rrbracket)
and we
set
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(l)\mathrel{\circ}\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d+l+2}\llbracket{B}\rrbracket=\llbracket{A}\rrbracket)
.

\blacktriangleright
If
M=\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{N}
then we have
{\Gamma}\vdash{N}:{{B}\Rightarrow{C}}
and
A=({\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{B}}\Rightarrow{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{C}})
and hence
\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,{\llbracket{B}\rrbracket}\Rightarrow{\llbracket{C}\rrbracket})
and
we set
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{int}}^{\llbracket{B}\rrbracket,\llbracket{C}\rrbracket}\mathrel{\circ}\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\llbracket{B}\rrbracket}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\llbracket{C}\rrbracket})=\llbracket{A}\rrbracket)
.

Assume now that
M=M_{0}+M_{1}
.
We distinguish the same subcases as in the proof of
Lemma
4.19
.

\blacktriangleright
The last rule of
\delta
is
2
so that
M_{j}=\operatorname{\pi}_{j}^{d}(N)
,
A=\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{B}
and
{\Gamma}\vdash{N}:{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d+1}{B}}
by a derivation
\delta^{\prime}
such that
\mathsf{sz}(\delta^{\prime})=\mathsf{sz}(\delta)-1
.
By inductive hypothesis we have
f=\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d+1}\llbracket{B}\rrbracket)
and we
know that the morphisms
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\mathrel{\circ}f=\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
and
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\mathrel{\circ}f=\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
are summable, with sum
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\sigma\mathrel{\circ}f
.
We set
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\sigma\mathrel{\circ}\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\llbracket{B}\rrbracket)
so that actually
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
.

\blacktriangleright
The last rule of
\delta
is
2
so that
M_{0}=\operatorname{\pi}_{1}^{d}(N_{0})
,
M_{1}=\operatorname{\pi}_{0}^{d}(N_{1})
,
A=\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{B}
and
{\Gamma}\vdash{N_{0}+N_{1}}:{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d+1}{B}}
by a derivation
\delta^{\prime}
such that
\mathsf{sz}(\delta^{\prime})=\mathsf{sz}(\delta)-1
.
By inductive hypothesis we have defined two summable morphisms
\llbracket{N_{j}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d+1}\llbracket{B}\rrbracket)
for

j=0,1
.
It follows that the 4 morphisms

(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i})\,\llbracket{N_{j}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
(for
i,j\in\{0,1\}
)
are summable, and hence
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\llbracket{N_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
and
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\mathrel{\circ}\llbracket{N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
are
summable.
We set
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
.

\blacktriangleright
The last rule of
\delta
is
2
so that there is a linear
context
L
of height
1
and terms
N_{0}
,
N_{1}
such that
M_{j}=L[N_{j}]
and
{\Gamma}\vdash{L[N_{0}+N_{1}]}:{A}
by a derivation
\delta^{\prime}
such that
\mathsf{sz}(\delta^{\prime})=\mathsf{sz}(\delta)-1
. This implies (by a simple inspection of the various possibilities for

L
which has height
1
) that for some context
\Delta
and some
type
B
one has
{\Delta}\vdash{N_{0}+N_{1}}:{B}
by a derivation
\delta^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathsf{sz}(\delta^{\prime\prime})=\mathsf{sz}(\delta^{\prime})-k_{L}
where
k_{L}\in{{\mathbb{N}}^{+}}

depends only on
L
(if for instance

L=\mathsf{if}^{d}([\ ],P_{0},P_{1})
then
k_{L}=1+k_{0}+k_{1}
where
k_{i}
is
the size of the typing derivation of
P_{i}
). Now we consider the various possibilities for
L
.

•

L=\lambda x^{C}\,{[\ ]}
and we have
\Delta=(\Gamma,x:B)
,
A=({C}\Rightarrow{B})
.
By inductive hypothesis we have
\llbracket{N_{j}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:C}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket}\mathrel{\&}{\llbracket{C}\rrbracket},\llbracket{B}\rrbracket)
for
j=0,1
,
\llbracket{N_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:C}
and
\llbracket{N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:C}
are summable and also that
\llbracket{N_{0}+N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:C}=\llbracket{N_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:C}+\llbracket{N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:C}
. We have
\llbracket{M_{j}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}{\llbracket{N_{j}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:C}}
because we know that there is a derivation
\delta_{j}
of
{\Gamma,x:C}\vdash{N_{j}}:{B}
such that
\mathsf{sz}(\delta_{j})\leq\mathsf{sz}(\delta)
by Lemma
4.19
.
It follows that
\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
and
\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
are summable and we can set
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

•

L=({[\ ]})\,{P}
and we have
\Delta=\Gamma
,
B=({C}\Rightarrow{A})
,
{\Gamma}\vdash{P}:{C}
by a derivation
of size
k_{P}>0
and the derivation
\delta^{\prime}
of
{\Gamma}\vdash{N_{0}+N_{1}}:{{C}\Rightarrow{A}}
satisfies
\mathsf{sz}(\delta)=\mathsf{sz}(\delta^{\prime})+k_{P}+1
.
So by inductive hypothesis
\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\llbracket{C}\rrbracket)
, and
\llbracket{N_{j}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,{\llbracket{C}\rrbracket}\Rightarrow{\llbracket{A}\rrbracket})
(for
j=0,1
) are summable and we have
\llbracket{N_{0}+N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{N_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
.
We have
\llbracket{M_{j}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\llbracket{N_{j}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}}\rangle
because the derivation
\delta_{j}
of
{\Gamma}\vdash{M_{j}}:{A}

satisfies
\mathsf{sz}(\delta_{j})\leq\mathsf{sz}(\delta)
and hence
\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
and
\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
are summable with
\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\llbracket{N_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}}\rangle
by left-linearity of
\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}
.
We set
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
.

•

L=\mathsf{if}^{d}([\ ],P_{0},P_{1})
and we have
\Delta=\Gamma
,
B=\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{\iota}
,
A=\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{C}
and
{\Gamma}\vdash{P_{i}}:{C}
for
i=0,1
by derivations of sizes
k_{0}
and
k_{1}
respectively so that, denoting by
\delta^{\prime}
the derivation of
{\Gamma}\vdash{N_{0}+N_{1}}:{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{\iota}}
, we have
\mathsf{sz}(\delta)=\mathsf{sz}(\delta^{\prime})+k_{0}+k_{1}+1
. It follows by inductive hypothesis that
\llbracket{P_{i}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\llbracket{C}\rrbracket)
for
i=0,1
, and that
\llbracket{N_{j}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\mathsf{N})
for
j=0,1

are summable with
\llbracket{N_{0}+N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{N_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
.
For
j=0,1
we have
\llbracket{M_{j}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{d}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\llbracket{N_{j}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\langle{\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}}\rangle}\rangle
because the derivation
\delta_{j}
of
{\Gamma}\vdash{M_{j}}:{A}

satisfies
\mathsf{sz}(\delta_{j})\leq\mathsf{sz}(\delta)
(by Lemma
4.19
) and hence, by left-linearity
of
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{d}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})
,
\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
and
\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
are summable and satisfy

\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{d}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\llbracket{N_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\langle{\llbracket{P_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\llbracket{P_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}}\rangle}\rangle
.
We set
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
.

•

L=\mathsf{let}^{d}(x,[\ ],P)
and we have
\Delta=\Gamma
,
B=\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{\iota}
,
{\Gamma,x:\iota}\vdash{P}:{C}
by a derivation of size
k
and

A=\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{C}
so that denoting by
\delta^{\prime}
the derivation of
{\Gamma}\vdash{N_{0}+N_{1}}:{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{\iota}}
we have
\mathsf{sz}(\delta)=\mathsf{sz}(\delta^{\prime})+k+1
.
This case is completely similar to the previous one.
By inductive hypothesis we have
\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{\iota}}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\mathsf{N},\llbracket{C}\rrbracket)
and, for

j=0,1
we have
\llbracket{N_{j}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{\mathsf{N}}{)}
which are
summable with
\llbracket{N_{0}+N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{N_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
.
For
j=0,1
we have
\llbracket{M_{j}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{d}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{let}})\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\llbracket{N_{j}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{\iota}}}\rangle
because the derivation
\delta_{j}
of
{\Gamma}\vdash{M_{j}}:{A}

satisfies
\mathsf{sz}(\delta_{j})\leq\mathsf{sz}(\delta)
(by Lemma
4.19
) and hence, by left-linearity
of
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{d}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{let}})
,
\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
and
\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
are summable and satisfy

\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{d}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{let}})\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\llbracket{N_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}{\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:C}}}\rangle
.
We set
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
.

•

L=\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{[\ ]}
and we have
\Delta=\Gamma
,
B=({C}\Rightarrow{E})
and

A=({\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{C}}\Rightarrow{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{E}})
and we use
\delta^{\prime}
for the derivation of
{\Gamma}\vdash{N_{0}+N_{1}}:{{C}\Rightarrow{E}}
so that
\mathsf{sz}(\delta)=\mathsf{sz}(\delta^{\prime})+1
and hence by inductive hypothesis we have
\llbracket{N_{j}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,{\llbracket{C}\rrbracket}\Rightarrow{\llbracket{E}\rrbracket})
for
j=0,1
, these two morphisms are summable and we have
\llbracket{N_{0}+N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{N_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
.
For
j=0,1
we have
\llbracket{M_{j}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{int}}^{\llbracket{C}\rrbracket,\llbracket{E}\rrbracket}\mathrel{\circ}\llbracket{N_{j}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
because the derivation
\delta_{j}
of
{\Gamma}\vdash{M_{j}}:{A}

satisfies
\mathsf{sz}(\delta_{j})\leq\mathsf{sz}(\delta)
(by Lemma
4.19
) and hence, by linearity of
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{int}}^{\llbracket{C}\rrbracket,\llbracket{E}\rrbracket}
,
\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
and
\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
are summable and satisfy

\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{int}}^{\llbracket{C}\rrbracket,\llbracket{E}\rrbracket}\mathrel{\circ}(\llbracket{N_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma})
.
We set
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
.

•

L=\operatorname{\pi}_{i}^{d}([\ ])
and we have
\Delta=\Gamma
,
B=\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d+1}{C}
and
A=\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{C}
and we use
\delta^{\prime}
for the derivation of
{\Gamma}\vdash{N_{0}+N_{1}}:{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d+1}{C}}
so that
\mathsf{sz}(\delta)=\mathsf{sz}(\delta^{\prime})+1
and hence by inductive hypothesis we have
\llbracket{N_{j}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d+1}\llbracket{C}\rrbracket)
for
j=0,1
, these two morphisms are summable and we have
\llbracket{N_{0}+N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{N_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
.
For
j=0,1
we have
\llbracket{M_{j}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}{\operatorname{\pi}_{i}}\mathrel{\circ}\llbracket{N_{j}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
because the derivation
\delta_{j}
of
{\Gamma}\vdash{M_{j}}:{A}

satisfies
\mathsf{sz}(\delta_{j})\leq\mathsf{sz}(\delta)
(by Lemma
4.19
) and hence, by linearity of
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}{\operatorname{\pi}_{i}}
,
\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
and
\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
are summable and satisfy

\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i}\mathrel{\circ}(\llbracket{N_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma})
. We set
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
.

•

The remaining cases are similar:
in each of them we see that we can sensibly set

\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
.

\displaystyle\llbracket{x_{i}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{pr}_{i}

\displaystyle\llbracket{\lambda x^{B}\,{N}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}{\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:B}}

\displaystyle\llbracket{({N})\,{P}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}}\rangle

\displaystyle\llbracket{\mathsf{Y}N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Y}^{\llbracket{A}\rrbracket}\mathrel{\circ}\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle\llbracket{\underline{n}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\overline{n}

\displaystyle\llbracket{\operatorname{\mathsf{succ}}^{d}(N)}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\overline{\mathsf{suc}}^{d}(\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma})

\displaystyle\llbracket{\operatorname{\mathsf{pred}}^{d}(N)}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\overline{\mathsf{suc}}^{d}(\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma})

\displaystyle\llbracket{\mathsf{if}^{d}(N,P_{0},P_{1})}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\overline{\mathsf{if}}^{d}(\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\llbracket{P_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\llbracket{P_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma})

\displaystyle\llbracket{\mathsf{let}^{d}(x,N,P)}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\overline{\mathsf{let}}^{d}(\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}{\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\iota}})

\displaystyle\llbracket{\operatorname{\pi}_{i}^{d}(N)}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i}\mathrel{\circ}\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle\llbracket{\operatorname{\iota}_{i}^{d}(N)}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\iota}_{i}\mathrel{\circ}\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle\llbracket{\theta^{d}(N)}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\theta\mathrel{\circ}\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle\llbracket{\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}^{d}_{l}(N)}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(l)\mathrel{\circ}\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle\llbracket{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{N}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{int}}^{\llbracket{B}\rrbracket,\llbracket{C}\rrbracket}\mathrel{\circ}\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle\llbracket{M_{0}+M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

Interpretation of terms (see the proof of
Theorem
5.55
)

5.6.1.
Substitution lemmas

The first substitution lemma is completely standard in a

\lambda
-calculus setting.

Lemma 5.57
.

If
{\Gamma}\vdash{M}:{B}
, so that
{\Gamma,x:A}\vdash{M}:{B}
, then we have
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:A}=\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\mathrel{\circ}\mathsf{pr}_{0}
where
\mathsf{pr}_{0}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket}\mathrel{\&}{\llbracket{A}\rrbracket},\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket)
is the
first projection.

Proof 5.58
.

By induction on the typing derivation of
M
.

Lemma 5.59

(Ordinary substitution)
.

If
{\Gamma}\vdash{N}:{A}
and
{\Gamma,x:A}\vdash{M}:{B}
then
\llbracket{{M}\left[{N}/{x}\right]}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\llbracket{B}\rrbracket)
satisfies

\displaystyle\llbracket{{M}\left[{x}/{N}\right]}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:A}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}}\rangle\,.

Proof 5.60
.

By induction on the typing derivation
M
.

Lemma 5.61

(Semantics of the differential)
.

If
{\Gamma,x:A}\vdash{M}:{B}
then
\llbracket{\partial({x},{M})}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A}}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\mathrel{\&}\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{\llbracket{A}\rrbracket},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\llbracket{B}\rrbracket)
satisfies

\displaystyle\llbracket{\partial({x},{M})}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A}}=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:A}\,.

If

\Gamma=(A_{1},\dots,A_{k})
and
\Delta=(A_{1},\dots,A_{i-1},\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A_{i}},A_{i+1},\dots,A_{k})
for some

i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
and
{\Gamma}\vdash{M}:{B}
, and then we have
{\Delta}\vdash{\partial({x_{i}},{M})}:{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{B}}
and in this slightly more
general situation the lemma states that
\llbracket{\partial({x_{i}},{M})}\rrbracket_{\Delta}=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{i}^{Z_{1},\dots,Z_{k}}{\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Delta}}
where
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{i}^{Z_{1},\dots,Z_{k}}f=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}f\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{i}_{Z_{1},\dots,Z_{k}}
is the “
i
th partial derivative of
f
”.
This slightly more general statement is equivalent to the lemma by the
symmetry of the cartesian product
\mathrel{\&}
.

Proof 5.62
.

By induction on
M
,
and not on its typing derivation
\delta
. This is possible thanks to Theorem
5.55
which
states that the interpretation does not depend on the typing
derivation. This point is crucial when dealing with sums. We use the
following notations:
f
for
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:A}
,
Z
for
\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket
,
U
for
\llbracket{A}\rrbracket
, and
X
for
\llbracket{B}\rrbracket
. Sometimes we also write
\Gamma=(x_{1}:A_{1},\dots,x_{k}:A_{k})
and in that
case we set
Z_{i}=\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket
so that
Z=Z_{1}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}Z_{k}
.

\blacktriangleright
Assume that
M=x
and hence
\delta
must end with
2
. We have
X=U
and
f=\mathsf{pr}_{1}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({Z}\mathrel{\&}{U},U)
, we have

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}f=(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\mathsf{pr}_{1})\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}=\mathsf{pr}_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\left({\operatorname{\iota}_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}U}\right)=\mathsf{pr}_{1}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({Z}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}U},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}U)
since
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}
commutes with cartesian products in

{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}
.
It follows that
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}f=\llbracket{\partial({x},{M})}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A}}
since
\partial({x},{M})=M
.

\blacktriangleright
Assume that
M=x_{i}
for some
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
and hence

\delta
must end with
2
So we have
f=\mathsf{pr}_{i}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z_{1}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}Z_{k}\mathrel{\&}U,Z_{i})
,
X=Z_{i}
and we have
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}f=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}f\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{Z,U}=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}f\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{k+1}_{Z_{1},\dots,Z_{k},U}=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\mathsf{pr}_{i}\mathrel{\circ}(\operatorname{\iota}_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\iota}_{0}\mathrel{\&}U)=\mathsf{pr}_{i}\mathrel{\circ}(\operatorname{\iota}_{0}\mathrel{\&}\cdots\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\iota}_{0}\mathrel{\&}U)=\operatorname{\iota}_{0}\mathrel{\circ}\mathsf{pr}_{i}
using the fact that
\operatorname{\mathsf{S}}
preserves cartesian products and the
expression (
10
) of the
k+1
-ary
\psi^{i}
. Therefore
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}f=\llbracket{\partial({x},{M})}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A}}
since
\partial({x},{M})=\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(x_{i})
.

\blacktriangleright
Assume that
M=\lambda y^{C}\,{P}
so that
\delta
must end with
2
applied to
{\Gamma,x:A,y:C}\vdash{P}:{E}
and we have
B=({C}\Rightarrow{E})
and hence
X=({V}\Rightarrow{Y})
where
\llbracket{C}\rrbracket=V
and
\llbracket{E}\rrbracket=Y
. Let
g=\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:A,y:C}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z\mathrel{\&}U\mathrel{\&}V,Y)
. Notice that

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}g=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}g\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{Z,U,V}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}U\mathrel{\&}V,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y)
. We have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}f

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}g)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}g)\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{Z,U}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}((\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}g)\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{0}_{{Z}\mathrel{\&}{U},V})\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{Z,U}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby Lemma~\ref{lemma:Sdfun-curry}}

where

\psi^{0}_{{Z}\mathrel{\&}{U},V}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Z\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}U\mathrel{\&}V,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Z\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}U\mathrel{\&}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}V)
. So

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}f=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}((\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}g)\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{0}_{{Z}\mathrel{\&}{U},V}\mathrel{\circ}({\psi^{1}_{Z,U}}\mathrel{\&}{V}))=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}((\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}g)\mathrel{\circ}\psi_{Z,U,V}^{1})=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}g)
. By inductive hypothesis we have
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}g=\llbracket{\partial({x},{P})}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A},y:C}
and hence
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}f=\llbracket{\lambda y^{C}\,{\partial({x},{P})}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A}}
as required.

\blacktriangleright
Assume that
M=\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{P}
so that
\delta
must end with
2
applied to
{\Gamma,x:A}\vdash{P}:{{C}\Rightarrow{E}}
and we have
B=({C}\Rightarrow{E})
and hence
X=({V}\Rightarrow{Y})
where
\llbracket{C}\rrbracket=V
and
\llbracket{E}\rrbracket=Y
. Let
g=\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:A}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({Z}\mathrel{\&}{U},{V}\Rightarrow{Y})
so that
f=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{cur}}g=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{int}}^{V,Y}\mathrel{\circ}g\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({Z}\mathrel{\&}{U},{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}V}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y})
where
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{int}}^{V,Y}=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}{(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{{V}\Rightarrow{Y},V}_{1})}
.
Then

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}f

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{cur}}g}\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{Z,U}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}{(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{{V}\Rightarrow{Y},V}_{1})}}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}g\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{Z,U}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{{V}\Rightarrow{Y},V}_{1})\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{0}_{{V}\Rightarrow{Y},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}V})\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}g\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby Lemma~\ref{lemma:Sdfun-curry}.}

Next we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{2}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\psi^{1}_{{V}\Rightarrow{Y},V}\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{0}_{{V}\Rightarrow{Y},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}V}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{2}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\psi^{0}_{{V}\Rightarrow{Y},V}\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{{V}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y},V}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{2}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\psi^{0}_{{V}\Rightarrow{Y},V}\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{{V}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y},V}

by Lemma
5.12
and naturality of
\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}
. By our
identification of
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}({V}\Rightarrow{Y})
with
{V}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y}

through the iso

\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}})
we have

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{0}_{{V}\Rightarrow{Y},V}=\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(({V}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y})\mathrel{\&}V,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y)
and hence

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}f

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{{V}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y},V})\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}g

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{{V}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y},V})\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}g

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{cur}}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}g

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{\partial({x},{P})})}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A}}\,.

Notice that we have identified
\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}_{{V}\Rightarrow{Y}}
with

{V}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}_{Y}}
in accordance with our convention of
identifying
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{2}({V}\Rightarrow{Y})
with
{V}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{2}Y}
.

\blacktriangleright
Assume that
M=({P})\,{Q}
so that
\delta
must end with
2
applied to
{\Gamma,x:A}\vdash{P}:{{C}\Rightarrow{B}}
and
{\Gamma,x:A}\vdash{Q}:{C}
and let
Y=\llbracket{C}\rrbracket
. Let
p=\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({Z}\mathrel{\&}{U},{Y}\Rightarrow{X})
and
q=\llbracket{Q}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({Z}\mathrel{\&}{U},Y)
so that
f=\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=({p})\,{q}=\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{p,q}\rangle\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({Z}\mathrel{\&}{U},X)
,
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}p\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({Z}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}U},{Y}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X})
and

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}q\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({Z}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}U},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y)
. We have

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{cur}}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}p}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({Z}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}U},{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{2}X})
so that
\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{cur}}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}p}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({Z}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}U},{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X})
and hence

({\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{cur}}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}p}})\,{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}q}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({Z}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}U},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X)
, we prove that

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}(({p})\,{q})=({\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{cur}}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}p}})\,{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}q}\,.

We have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}(({p})\,{q})

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}})\mathrel{\circ}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\langle{p,q}\rangle)\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{Z,U}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}})\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}p,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}q}\rangle\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{Z,U}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptsince $\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}$ preserves cart.~prod.}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}})\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}p,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}q}\rangle\,.

where

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({({X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y})}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y)

and we know by Lemma
5.34
that

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}=\theta\mathrel{\circ}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}^{\prime})\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{{Y}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X},Y}
where
\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}^{\prime}
is the
evaluation morphism in

{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({({Y}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X})}\mathrel{\&}{Y},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X)
.

On the other hand

\displaystyle({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{cur}}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}p}})\,{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}q}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{cur}}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}p},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}q}\rangle

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\left({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{int}}^{Y,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\right)\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}p,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}q}\rangle

where we recall that

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{int}}^{Y,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}((\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}^{\prime})\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{{Y}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X},Y})\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({Y}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X},{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{2}X})
and we have used Equation (
12
).
Therefore

\displaystyle({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{cur}}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}p}})\,{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}q}=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}^{\prime}\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{{Y}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X},Y}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}p,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}q}\rangle\,.

It follows that

\displaystyle({\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{cur}}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}p}})\,{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}q}

\displaystyle=\theta\mathrel{\circ}({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{cur}}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}p}})\,{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}q}

\displaystyle=\theta\mathrel{\circ}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}^{\prime})\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{{Y}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X},Y}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}p,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}q}\rangle

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}p,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}q}\rangle\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby Lemma~\ref{lemma:sdfun-Ev-expression}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}f

where the first equation results from our identification of
{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}Y}\Rightarrow{\theta}
with
\theta
and we have also used the fact that

f=({p})\,{q}=\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{p,q}\rangle
. Finally, using again Equation (
12
),

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}f

\displaystyle=({\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{int}}\mathrel{\circ}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}p}})\,{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}q}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{({\theta(\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{\partial({x},{P})})})\,{\partial({x},{Q})}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A}}

as contended, using of course also the inductive hypothesis.

\blacktriangleright
Assume that
M=\operatorname{\mathsf{succ}}^{d}(P)
so that the last rule of
\delta
is
2
and that we have
{\Gamma,x:A}\vdash{P}:{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{\iota}=B}
and hence
X=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\mathsf{N}
. Let

so that

, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}f

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{suc}})\mathrel{\circ}f)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d+1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{suc}})\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}g\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{Z,U}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d+1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{suc}})\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}g

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d+1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{suc}})\mathrel{\circ}\llbracket{\partial({x},{P})}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A}}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby inductive hypothesis}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\operatorname{\mathsf{succ}}^{d+1}(\partial({x},{P}))}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A}}

The cases where

\operatorname{\iota}_{i}^{d}(P)

\blacktriangleright

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{d}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{X}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{p,\langle{q_{0},q_{1}}\rangle}\rangle)

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{d}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{X}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\langle{p,\langle{q_{0},q_{1}}\rangle}\rangle\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{Z,U}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby def.~of }\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}\text{ and functoriality of }\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt=\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{d+1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{X}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}p,\langle{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}q_{0},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}q_{1}}\rangle}\rangle\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby Theorem~\ref{th:sdfun-sdfunpart-sfunadd}}

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt=\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(d)\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{d+1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}p,\langle{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}q_{0},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}q_{1}}\rangle}\rangle\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby Lemma~\ref{lemma:sif-slet-sdfunpart}}

therefore

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}f

\displaystyle=\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(d)\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{d+1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}p,\langle{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}q_{0},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}q_{1}}\rangle}\rangle

\displaystyle=\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(d)\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{d+1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})_{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\llbracket{\partial({x},{P})}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A}},\langle{\llbracket{\partial({x},{Q_{0}})}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A}},\llbracket{\partial({x},{Q_{1}})}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A}}}\rangle}\rangle

by ind. hyp.

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\theta(\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}_{d}(\mathsf{if}^{d+1}(\partial({x},{P}),\partial({x},{Q_{0}}),\partial({x},{Q_{1}}))))}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\partial({x},{M})}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A}}

as required.
The case
M=\mathsf{let}^{d}(y,P,Q)
is completely similar.

\blacktriangleright
Assume that
M=M_{0}+M_{1}
. Whatever the last rule of
\delta
is, we
know that
({\Gamma,x:A}\vdash{M_{i}}:{B})_{i=0,1}

, where

\llbracket{\partial({x},{M_{i}})}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=g_{i}

for
i=0,1
.
By left-linearity of composition in
{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}
, we have

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle=g_{0}+g_{1}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\partial({x},{M_{0}})}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A}}+\llbracket{\partial({x},{M_{0}})}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\partial({x},{M_{0}})+\partial({x},{M_{1}})}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A}}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby Theorem~\ref{th:sem-defined-sum}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\partial({x},{M})}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A}}

as required.

\blacktriangleright
Assume that
M=\mathsf{Y}P
so that
\delta

2
and that we have
{\Gamma,x:A}\vdash{P}:{{B}\Rightarrow{B}}
so that, setting
g=\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:A}
we have
g\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({Z}\mathrel{\&}{U},{X}\Rightarrow{X})
and
f=\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:A}=\mathcal{Y}^{X}\mathrel{\circ}g
.
We have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}f

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}{\mathcal{Y}^{X}}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}g

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Y}^{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}^{X,X})\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}g\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby Theorem~\ref{th:sdfun-sfix}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Y}^{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}^{X,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{{X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X},X})\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}g\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby Lemma~\ref{lemma:sdfun-Ev-expression}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Y}^{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\mathrel{\circ}{({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\Rightarrow{\theta})}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}^{X,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{{X}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X},X})\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}g\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby cartesian closedness}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Y}^{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\mathrel{\circ}{({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\Rightarrow{\theta})}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{int}}^{X,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}g\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby definition of }\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{int}}^{X,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Y}^{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\mathrel{\circ}\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{int}}^{X,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}g

and hence

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}f

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Y}^{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\mathrel{\circ}\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{int}}^{X,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}g

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Y}^{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\mathrel{\circ}\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{int}}^{X,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}X}\mathrel{\circ}{\llbracket{\partial({x},{P})}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A}}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\mathsf{Y}(\theta(\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{\partial({x},{P})}))}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\partial({x},{M})}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{A}}

as required.

5.6.2.
Soundness theorem

Theorem 5.63

(Soundness of the semantics)
.

Assume that
{\Gamma}\vdash{M}:{A}
and
M\mathrel{\to_{\Lambda_{\mathsf{cd}}}}M^{\prime}
(so that

{\Gamma}\vdash{M^{\prime}}:{A}
). Then we have
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{M^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
.

Proof 5.64
.

We consider the various cases in the definition of
\mathrel{\to_{\Lambda_{\mathsf{cd}}}}
. We set

Z=\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket
and
X=\llbracket{A}\rrbracket
.

\blacktriangleright
Assume first that
M\to_{\mathsf{lin}}M^{\prime}
. The fact that
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{M^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
results simply from the linearity of
the semantic constructs corresponding to the linear contexts. As
an example, consider the situation where
M=\mathsf{let}^{d}(x,M_{0}+M_{1},N)
and
M^{\prime}=\mathsf{let}^{d}(x,M_{0},N)+\mathsf{let}^{d}(x,M_{1},N)
(so that we have
{\Gamma}\vdash{M_{i}}:{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{\iota}}
and
{\Gamma}\vdash{N}:{B}
for a type
B
, and we have
A=\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{B}
). Then
we have

\displaystyle\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{d}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{let}})\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\llbracket{M_{0}+M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{d}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{let}})\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{d}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{let}})\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}}\rangle+\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{d}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{let}})\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}}\rangle

by the bilinearity of
\overline{\mathsf{let}}
.

\blacktriangleright
Assume that
M=({\lambda x^{A}\,{P}})\,{Q}
and
M^{\prime}={P}\left[{Q}/{x}\right]
, we directly apply Lemma
5.59
.

\blacktriangleright
Assume that
M=\mathsf{Y}N
and
M^{\prime}=({N})\,{M}
. We directly apply Equation (
5
).

\blacktriangleright
Assume that
M=\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{(}{\lambda x^{B}\,{P}})
and
M^{\prime}=\lambda x^{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{B}}\,{\partial({x},{P})}
so
that
A=({\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{B}}\Rightarrow{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{C}})
and
{\Gamma,x:B}\vdash{P}:{C}
, so that
setting
U=\llbracket{B}\rrbracket
and
Y=\llbracket{C}\rrbracket
we have

f=\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:B}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({Z}\mathrel{\&}{U},Y)
. Then we have

\displaystyle\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{\mathsf{int}}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}f

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}^{U,Y}\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{{U}\Rightarrow{Y},U})\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}f

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}^{U,Y}\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{{U}\Rightarrow{Y},U}\mathrel{\circ}\left({\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}f}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}U}\right))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}^{U,Y}\mathrel{\circ}\left({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}f)}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}U}\right)\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{Z,U})\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby nat.~of }\psi^{1}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}^{U,Y}\mathrel{\circ}\left({\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}f}\mathrel{\&}{U}\right))\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{Z,U})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}f\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{1}_{Z,U})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{1}f)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\llbracket{\partial({x},{P})}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{B}})\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby Lemma~\ref{lemma:sem-diff-subst}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\lambda x^{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}{B}}\,{\partial({x},{P})}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\blacktriangleright
Assume that
M=\mathsf{if}^{0}(\underline{\nu+1},P_{0},P_{1})
and
M^{\prime}=P_{1}
so that,
setting
p_{i}=\llbracket{P_{i}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,X)
for

, we have
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\overline{\nu+1}\mathrel{\circ}0,\langle{p_{0},p_{1}}\rangle}\rangle=p_{1}
by the second diagram
of (
11
), where
0
is the unique element of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,\top)
. The cases where

,

, and

are similar.

\blacktriangleright
Assume that
M=\mathsf{let}^{0}(x,\underline{\nu},P)
and
M^{\prime}={P}\left[{\underline{\nu}}/{x}\right]
. Let
f=\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:\iota}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({Z}\mathrel{\&}{\mathsf{N}},X)

We have
\overline{\nu}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,\mathsf{N})
and

h_{\mathsf{N}}\,\overline{\nu}=\overline{\nu}^{!}
(actually
\overline{\nu}
is a
morphism in the Eilenberg-Moore category of
\oc{\_}

\displaystyle\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\overline{\mathsf{let}}^{0}(\overline{\nu},f)\text{\hskip 10.22217ptusing the notation of
Section~\ref{sec:syn-basic-constr}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{let}})\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\overline{\nu},\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}f}\rangle

\displaystyle=\overline{\mathsf{let}}\,({\overline{\nu}}\otimes{\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}f})\,\operatorname{\mathsf{contr}}_{Z}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{ev}}\,\gamma\,({h_{\mathsf{N}}}\otimes{\left({\oc{\mathsf{N}}}\multimap{X}\right)})({\overline{\nu}}\otimes{\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}f})\,\operatorname{\mathsf{contr}}_{Z}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{ev}}\,({f}\otimes{\overline{\nu}^{!}})\,\operatorname{\mathsf{contr}}_{Z}

\displaystyle=f\mathrel{\circ}\langle{Z,\overline{\nu}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\llbracket{{P}\left[{\underline{\nu}}/{x}\right]}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

by
Lemma
5.59
.

\blacktriangleright
Assume that
M=\operatorname{\pi}_{j}^{d}(\lambda x^{B}\,{P})
and
M^{\prime}=\lambda x^{B}\,{\operatorname{\pi}_{i}^{d}(P)}
so that we must have
{\Gamma,x:B}\vdash{P}:{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d+1}{C}}
with

,
X=({U}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}Y})
where

and
Y=\llbracket{C}\rrbracket
. Let
f=\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:B}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({Z}\mathrel{\&}{U},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d+1}Y)
so that
\llbracket{\lambda x^{B}\,{P}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,{U}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d+1}Y})

and
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i}\mathrel{\circ}f\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}({Z}\mathrel{\&}{U},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}Y)
.
Remember that, for any object
V
,

\operatorname{\varphi}^{0}_{{U}\Rightarrow{V},\oc{U}}\in\mathcal{L}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}{\left({\oc{U}}\multimap{V}\right)}\otimes\oc{X},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}({\left({\oc{X}}\multimap{U}\right)}\otimes{\oc{X}}))

and that by (
S
\otimes
-fun
) of
[
Ehr23
]
the morphism
\operatorname{\varphi}^{\multimap}=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}({(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}\operatorname{\mathsf{ev}})\,\operatorname{\varphi}^{0}_{{U}\Rightarrow{V},\oc{U}}})\in\mathcal{L}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}({U}\Rightarrow{V}),{U}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}V})
is an iso. Thanks to this iso we identify the objects
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d+1}({U}\Rightarrow{Y})
and
{U}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d+1}Y}
. Under this identification the morphisms

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\in\mathcal{L}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d+1}({U}\Rightarrow{Y}),\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}({U}\Rightarrow{Y}))

and
{U}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{j}}\in\mathcal{L}({U}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d+1}Y},{U}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}Y})
are identified as well. Since
({U}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{j}})\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}f=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\mathrel{\circ}f)
we have

\displaystyle\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i}(\llbracket{\lambda x^{B}\,{P}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma})

\displaystyle=({U}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{j}})\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}f

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\mathrel{\circ}f)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\mathrel{\circ}\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:B})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Cur}}(\llbracket{\operatorname{\pi}_{i}^{d}(P)}\rrbracket_{\Gamma,x:B})

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\lambda x^{B}\,{\operatorname{\pi}_{i}^{d}(P)}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

as required.

\blacktriangleright
Assume that
M=\operatorname{\pi}_{i}^{d}(({P})\,{Q})
and
M^{\prime}=({\operatorname{\pi}_{i}^{d}(P)})\,{Q}
so that we must have
{\Gamma}\vdash{P}:{{B}\Rightarrow{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d+1}{C}}}
and
{\Gamma}\vdash{Q}:{B}
with
A=\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{C}
. Setting
U=\llbracket{B}\rrbracket
,
Y=\llbracket{C}\rrbracket
,
p=\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
and
q=\llbracket{Q}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
we have
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i}\mathrel{\circ}({p})\,{q}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d}{Y})
.
Then, by naturality of
evaluation, we have

\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{j})\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{p,q}\rangle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\left({({U}\Rightarrow{(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{j})})}\mathrel{\&}{U}\right)\mathrel{\circ}\langle{p,q}\rangle=\operatorname{\mathsf{Ev}}\mathrel{\circ}\langle{(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d+1}\operatorname{\pi}_{j})\mathrel{\circ}p,q}\rangle

under the same identification as in the previous case. That is
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{M^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
.

\blacktriangleright
Assume
M=\operatorname{\pi}_{j}^{d}(\operatorname{\mathsf{succ}}^{e}(P))
and
M^{\prime}=\operatorname{\mathsf{succ}}^{e-1}(\operatorname{\pi}_{j}^{d}(P))
so that we must have
d<e
,
{\Gamma}\vdash{\operatorname{\mathsf{succ}}^{e}(P)}:{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{e}{\iota}}

and
A=\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{e-1}{\iota}
.
We have
\overline{\mathsf{suc}}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathsf{N},\mathsf{N})
that we consider as usual as a
morphism in
{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\mathsf{N},\mathsf{N})
so that

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}\overline{\mathsf{suc}}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}\mathsf{N},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}\mathsf{N})
and since
d<e

we can write
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}\mathsf{N}=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d+1}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e-d-1}\mathsf{N}
so that

\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}\mathsf{N},\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e-1}\mathsf{N})
and we
have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}\overline{\mathsf{suc}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}(\operatorname{\pi}_{j}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e-d}\overline{\mathsf{suc}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e-d-1}\overline{\mathsf{suc}}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\pi}_{j})\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby nat.~of }\operatorname{\pi}_{j}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e-1}\overline{\mathsf{suc}}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{j}

so that
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{M^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
. The cases
M=\operatorname{\pi}_{j}^{d}(\operatorname{\mathsf{pred}}^{e}(P))
and
M^{\prime}=\operatorname{\mathsf{pred}}^{e-1}(\operatorname{\pi}_{j}(P))
(still with
d<e
) are completely similar.

\blacktriangleright
Assume that
M=\operatorname{\pi}_{i}^{d}(\mathsf{if}^{e}(N,P_{0},P_{1}))
and
M^{\prime}=\mathsf{if}^{e-1}(\operatorname{\pi}_{i}^{d}(N),P_{0},P_{1})
with
d<e
. We must have
{\Gamma}\vdash{N}:{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{e}{\iota}}
and
{\Gamma}\vdash{P_{j}}:{B}
for
j=0,1
, for a type
B
uniquely determined
by
A=\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{e-1}{B}
. Let
Y=\llbracket{B}\rrbracket
, we have
X=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e-1}Y
. Let
f=\llbracket{N}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}\mathsf{N})
and
p_{j}=\llbracket{P_{j}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(Z,Y)
for
j=0,1
.
We have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{e}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\mathrel{\circ}\langle{f,\langle{p_{0},p_{1}}\rangle}\rangle

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{0}_{\mathsf{N},{Y}\mathrel{\&}{Y}}(e)\mathrel{\circ}\langle{f,\langle{p_{0},p_{1}}\rangle}\rangle\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby Lemma~\ref{lemma:sdfunpart-it-expr}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}(\operatorname{\pi}_{i}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e-d}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}}))\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{0}_{\mathsf{N},{Y}\mathrel{\&}{Y}}(e)\mathrel{\circ}\langle{f,\langle{p_{0},p_{1}}\rangle}\rangle\,.

Now we have to consider the two cases
i=0
and
i=1
. The first case
is dealt with as follows.

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{0}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{e}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\mathrel{\circ}\langle{f,\langle{p_{0},p_{1}}\rangle}\rangle

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e-d-1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\mathrel{\circ}\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\right))\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{0}_{\mathsf{N},{Y}\mathrel{\&}{Y}}(e)\mathrel{\circ}\langle{f,\langle{p_{0},p_{1}}\rangle}\rangle

by Theorems
5.38

and
5.42

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e-1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\mathrel{\circ}\left({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\right)\mathrel{\circ}\left({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}\mathsf{N}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e)}\right)\mathrel{\circ}\langle{f,\langle{p_{0},p_{1}}\rangle}\rangle

by Lemma
5.22

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e-1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\mathrel{\circ}\left({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e-1)}\right)\mathrel{\circ}\langle{f,\langle{p_{0},p_{1}}\rangle}\rangle\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby Lemma~\ref{lemma:proj-sin-deep}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e-1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\mathrel{\circ}\left({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e-1}\mathsf{N}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e-1)}\right)\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\mathrel{\circ}f,\langle{p_{0},p_{1}}\rangle}\rangle

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{e-1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\mathrel{\circ}f,\langle{p_{0},p_{1}}\rangle}\rangle\,.

Let us deal with the second case.

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{e}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\mathrel{\circ}\langle{f,\langle{p_{0},p_{1}}\rangle}\rangle

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e-d-1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\mathrel{\circ}\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\right))\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{0}_{\mathsf{N},{X}\mathrel{\&}{X}}(e)\mathrel{\circ}\langle{f,\langle{p_{0},p_{1}}\rangle}\rangle

\displaystyle\ +\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e-d-1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\mathrel{\circ}\left({\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}\right))\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{0}_{\mathsf{N},{X}\mathrel{\&}{X}}(e)\mathrel{\circ}\langle{f,\langle{p_{0},p_{1}}\rangle}\rangle

by Theorems

and
5.42
.

By Lemmas
5.22
and
5.25

we have
\left({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}{\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}}\right)\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{\mathsf{N},{X}\mathrel{\&}{X}}_{0}(e)=\left({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}{\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e-1)}\right)
and
\left({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}{\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}}\right)\mathrel{\circ}\psi^{\mathsf{N},{X}\mathrel{\&}{X}}_{0}(e)=\left({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}{\operatorname{\pi}_{0}}}\mathrel{\&}{0}\right)
.
It follows by bilinearity of
\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e-1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})
that

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{e}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\mathrel{\circ}\langle{f,\langle{p_{0},p_{1}}\rangle}\rangle

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e-1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\mathrel{\circ}\left({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{1}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e-1)}\right)\mathrel{\circ}\langle{f,\langle{p_{0},p_{1}}\rangle}\rangle

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{e-1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{1}\mathrel{\circ}f,\langle{p_{0},p_{1}}\rangle}\rangle\,.

To summarize, for
i=0,1
and if
d<e
we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{e}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\mathrel{\circ}\langle{f,\langle{p_{0},p_{1}}\rangle}\rangle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{e-1}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\mathrel{\circ}\langle{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i}\mathrel{\circ}f,\langle{p_{0},p_{1}}\rangle}\rangle\,.

It follows that
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{M^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
.

The case
M=\operatorname{\pi}_{i}^{d}(\mathsf{let}^{e}(x,M,P))
and

M^{\prime}=\mathsf{let}^{e-1}(x,\operatorname{\pi}_{i}^{d}(M),P)

is handled similarly.

\blacktriangleright
Assume that

. We must have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\mathrel{\circ}\left({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}\mathsf{N}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e)}\right)\mathrel{\circ}\left({\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}\mathsf{N}}\mathrel{\&}{\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d-e}\operatorname{\pi}_{i}}\right)\mathrel{\circ}\langle{f,\langle{p_{0},p_{1}}\rangle}\rangle\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby nat.~of }\operatorname{\iota}_{0}(e)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}_{0}^{e}\mathsf{m}(\overline{\mathsf{if}})\mathrel{\circ}\langle{f,\langle{(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d-e}\operatorname{\pi}_{i})\mathrel{\circ}p_{0},(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d-e}\operatorname{\pi}_{i})\mathrel{\circ}p_{1}}\rangle}\rangle

and it follows that
\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{M^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
since

for

j=0,1
.

The case where
M=\operatorname{\pi}_{i}^{d}(\mathsf{let}^{e}(x,M,P))
and

\blacktriangleright
Assume that

and

{\Gamma}\vdash{N}:{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{d+2}{B}}

\displaystyle(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{0})\mathrel{\circ}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\theta)\mathrel{\circ}f

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}(\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\mathrel{\circ}\theta)\mathrel{\circ}f

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}(\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\pi}_{0})\mathrel{\circ}f

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{0})\mathrel{\circ}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{0})\mathrel{\circ}f

\displaystyle=\llbracket{M^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\,.

Notice that we are using the fact that we are composing

morphisms in
{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}
so that the equation

\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\mathrel{\circ}\theta=\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\pi}_{0}

\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\tau=\operatorname{\pi}_{0}\,\operatorname{\pi}_{0}

\mathcal{L}
.

The case where

in

.

\blacktriangleright
Assume that

\displaystyle(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i})\mathrel{\circ}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}\theta)\mathrel{\circ}f

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e-d-1}\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\pi}_{i})\mathrel{\circ}f\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby naturality of }\operatorname{\pi}_{i}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e-1}\theta)\mathrel{\circ}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i})\mathrel{\circ}f

\displaystyle=\llbracket{M^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\,.

\blacktriangleright

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}\theta\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d+1}\operatorname{\pi}_{i}\mathrel{\circ}f

\blacktriangleright

\displaystyle=\llbracket{M^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\,.

\blacktriangleright

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}((\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d-e}\operatorname{\pi}_{i})\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(l))\mathrel{\circ}f

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}((\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{l+2}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d-e-l-2}\operatorname{\pi}_{i})\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(l))\mathrel{\circ}f

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}(\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(l)\mathrel{\circ}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d-e}\operatorname{\pi}_{i}))\mathrel{\circ}f\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby naturality of }\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(l)

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(l))\mathrel{\circ}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i})\mathrel{\circ}f

\displaystyle=\llbracket{M^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\,.

\blacktriangleright
Assume that
M=\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{l+1}}^{d}(\cdots\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{0}}^{d}(\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}^{d}_{l}(N)))
and

and

\displaystyle\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{l+1}})\mathrel{\circ}\cdots\mathrel{\circ}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{0}})\mathrel{\circ}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\mathsf{c}}(l))\mathrel{\circ}f

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{0}})\mathrel{\circ}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{l+1}})\mathrel{\circ}\cdots\mathrel{\circ}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i_{1}})\mathrel{\circ}f

\displaystyle=\llbracket{M^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

by Lemma

.

\blacktriangleright

if

. We must have

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}(\operatorname{\pi}_{i}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\iota}_{j})\mathrel{\circ}f

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\delta}_{{i},{j}}f

since
\operatorname{\pi}_{i}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\iota}_{j}=\boldsymbol{\delta}_{{i},{j}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Id}}
.

\blacktriangleright
Assume that
M=\operatorname{\pi}_{i}^{d}(\operatorname{\iota}_{j}^{e}(N))
and

. Let

and

\displaystyle\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i})\mathrel{\circ}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e}\operatorname{\iota}_{j})\mathrel{\circ}f

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}(\operatorname{\pi}_{i}\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e-d}\operatorname{\iota}_{j})\mathrel{\circ}f

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}((\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e-d-1}\operatorname{\iota}_{j})\mathrel{\circ}\operatorname{\pi}_{i})\mathrel{\circ}f\text{\hskip 10.22217ptby naturality of }\operatorname{\pi}_{i}

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{e-1}\operatorname{\iota}_{j})\mathrel{\circ}(\operatorname{\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}}^{d}\operatorname{\pi}_{i})\mathrel{\circ}f

\displaystyle=\llbracket{M^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\,.

\blacktriangleright
Assume that
M=\operatorname{\pi}_{i}^{d}(\operatorname{\iota}_{j}^{e}(N))

\blacktriangleright

\displaystyle=\llbracket{M^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\,.

Definition 5.65
.

Let
S=[{M_{1},\dots,M_{k}}]\in\underline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{fin}}({\Lambda_{\mathsf{cd}}})}
and assume that
{\Gamma}\vdash{S}:{A}
, that is
({\Gamma}\vdash{M_{i}}:{A})_{i=1}^{k}
.
We say that
S
is
\mathcal{L}
-summable
if the family
(\llbracket{M_{i}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma})_{i=1}^{k}
is summable in

{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\llbracket{A}\rrbracket)
.
When this property holds we set
\llbracket{S}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\llbracket{M_{i}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\oc}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\llbracket{A}\rrbracket)
.

Notice that this is not a purely syntactic notion, it depends on the
notion of summability of
\mathcal{L}
.

Remark 5.66
.

We can introduce an absolute notion of summability by quantifying
universally on
\mathcal{L}
but this will not be really useful here.

Theorem 5.67

(multiset soundness)
.

If
S\in\underline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{fin}}({\Lambda_{\mathsf{cd}}})}
is such that
{\Gamma}\vdash{S}:{A}
and
S
is
\mathcal{L}
-summable and if

S\mathrel{\to_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{fin}}({\Lambda_{\mathsf{cd}}})}}S^{\prime}
then
S^{\prime}
is
\mathcal{L}
-summable and
\llbracket{S^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{S}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
.

Remember that
{\Gamma}\vdash{S^{\prime}}:{A}
by
Theorem
4.21
.

Proof 5.68
.

The following cases are possible, for some
S_{0}=[{N_{1},\dots,N_{k}}]
.

\blacktriangleright

S=S_{0}+[{M}]
,
M\mathrel{\to_{\Lambda_{\mathsf{cd}}}}0
and
S^{\prime}=S_{0}
. Since
(\llbracket{N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\dots,\llbracket{N_{k}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma})
is a
summable family, we know that the family

(\llbracket{N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\dots,\llbracket{N_{k}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma})
is summable by Theorem 5
of
[
Ehr23
]
. Moreover
\llbracket{S^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{S}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
because

\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{0}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=0
by
Theorem
5.63
.

\blacktriangleright

S=S_{0}+[{M}]
,
M\mathrel{\to_{\Lambda_{\mathsf{cd}}}}M^{\prime}
and
S^{\prime}=S_{0}+[{M^{\prime}}]
. Then we have
\llbracket{M^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
by
Theorem
5.63
and hence
S^{\prime}
is

\mathcal{L}
-summable and
\llbracket{S^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{S}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
.

\blacktriangleright

S=S_{0}+[{M}]
,
M\mathrel{\to_{\Lambda_{\mathsf{cd}}}}M_{0}+M_{1}
and
S^{\prime}=S_{0}+[{M_{0},M_{1}}]
. Then by
Theorem
5.63
we know that

\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
are summable and

\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
. By
Theorem 5 of
[
Ehr23
]

we know that
(\llbracket{N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\dots,\llbracket{N_{k}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma},\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma})
is a summable family with

\llbracket{S}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{N_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\cdots+\llbracket{N_{k}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{M_{0}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}+\llbracket{M_{1}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
, that is
S^{\prime}
is
\mathcal{L}
-summable and
\llbracket{S^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}=\llbracket{S}\rrbracket_{\Gamma}
.

Remark 5.69
.

This semantic notion of multiset summability seems counter to the
general philosophy of this work which is to take the notion of summability
into account at a purely syntactic level.
We hope to be able to avoid it by means of a “parallel reduction”
as explained in Section
4.3.1
; this does not seem
trivial and is postponed to further work since it is not strictly
necessary for our current purpose.
